\displaystyle\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}V^{3}_{3}f\hskip 1.42262pt\mathcal{D}\alpha_{AB}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}p^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptV^{2}_{3}f

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}|\mathcal{D}\alpha||u^{\prime}|^{-2}||V^{3}_{3}f||_{L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}||V^{2}_{3}f||_{L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{0}^{1}|u^{\prime}|^{-2}||\mathcal{D}\alpha||_{L^{4}(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})}||V^{3}_{3}f||_{L^{2}_{(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}}||V^{2}_{3}f||_{L^{4}_{(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}}.

(1.74)

Now we control
||\mathcal{D}\alpha||_{L^{4}(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})}
by codimension
1
trace inequality and control
||V^{2}_{3}f||_{L^{4}_{(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}}
by Sobolev embedding on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
:

\displaystyle||V^{2}_{3}f||_{L^{4}_{(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}}\lesssim\left[\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\mathcal{D}\left(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V^{2}_{3}f|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}+l.o.t.

(1.75)

Expressing the action of
\mathcal{D}
in terms of the action of the vector fields
V
, one obtains

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\left(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V^{2}_{3}f|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{\int_{P}V^{2}_{3}f\hskip 1.42262ptVV^{2}_{3}f}{\sqrt{\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V^{2}_{3}f|^{2}}}+l.o.t.\lesssim||VV^{2}_{3}f||_{L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}+l.o.t.

(1.76)

More rigorously, one may consider
\mathcal{D}\left(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V^{2}_{3}f|^{2}+\epsilon\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
and take the limit
\epsilon\to 0
. Therefore, one obtains

\displaystyle||V^{2}_{3}f||_{L^{4}_{(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}}\lesssim\left(\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}||VV^{2}_{3}f||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+l.o.t.

(1.77)

Finally, collecting all the terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{0}^{1}|u^{\prime}|^{-2}||\mathcal{D}\alpha||_{L^{4}(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})}||V^{3}_{3}f||_{L^{2}_{(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}}||V^{2}_{3}f||_{L^{4}_{(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}})L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}|u^{\prime}|^{-2}\sup_{\underline{u}}||\mathcal{D}^{2}\alpha||_{L^{2}(H_{u,\underline{u}})}\int_{0}^{1}\left[\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V^{3}f|^{2}d\mathcal{P}_{x}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}+\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|VV^{2}_{3}f|^{2}d\mathcal{P}_{x}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}\right]+l.o.t.

(1.78)

Note that all the terms can be estimated with the available regularity. Any attempt to lower the regularity level of the curvature would upset the delicate balance in controlling the top-order Vlasov norm.

This difficulty is sharply contrasted with other matter models such as the Yang–Mills equations coupled to Einstein, wherein the energy-momentum tensor is hyperbolic and possesses a better compatibility with the curvature structure. In those settings, the trace norms of the null curvature components are controllable, and one-derivative estimates on the Weyl tensor suffice to propagate two derivatives of the matter field; see
[
22
]
for the relevant analysis.

Thus, the Einstein–Vlasov system presents a genuinely new analytic challenge. While the failure of closure at this critical level (note that this ‘critical’ is unrelated to the criticality of the norm required for the Einstein’s equations in
3+1
dimensions) seems to preclude a direct extension of the characteristic Cauchy framework, it remains conceivable that a novel method—possibly circumventing the need for trace control of
\alpha
or exploiting the structure of the Vlasov equation in a more refined way—could provide an alternate resolution to the characteristic initial value problem in this setting.

1.8
Acknowledgements

P.M. is supported by the Center of Mathematical Sciences and Applications of Mathematics Department at Harvard University and Beijing Institute of Mathematical Sciences and Applications of Yau Mathematical Science Center at Tsinghua University.

2
Basic setup

2.1
Construction of the double null gauge

We begin by assuming that the functions
u
and
v
satisfy

g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}u\partial_{\nu}u=0,\hskip 5.69054ptg^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\underline{u}\partial_{\nu}\underline{u}=0.

Define null vector fields

L^{\mu}=-2g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}u,\hskip 5.69054pt\underline{L}^{\mu}=-2g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}\underline{u}.

Also define
\Omega>0
by

\Omega^{-2}=-\frac{1}{2}g(L,\underline{L}).

To define
(\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
on the spacetime, we proceed as follows. Let
(\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
be a coordinate system in some open subset
U_{1}
of the initial sphere
S_{u_{\infty},0}.
Define
(\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
on
\begin{Bmatrix}u=u_{\infty}\end{Bmatrix}
by solving
L(\theta^{A})=0,\hskip 2.84526ptA=1,2
and then extend to
u>u_{\infty}
by solving
\underline{L}(\theta^{A})=0,\hskip 2.84526ptA=1,2.

As such, we have defined coordinates
(u,v,\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
on a region
D(U_{1})
, defined as the image of
U_{1}
under the diffeomorphisms of
L
on
\begin{Bmatrix}u=u_{\infty}\end{Bmatrix}
and then the diffeomorphisms generated by
\underline{L}
. By repeating the procedure for a complementary open set
U_{2}
of
S_{u_{\infty},0}
such that
U_{1}\cup U_{2}=S_{u_{\infty},0}
, we get coordinate charts that cover the entire spacetime region that will be of interest. The spheres of constant
u
and
v
will be denoted by
S_{u,v}
and the restriction of
g
on these spheres will be denoted by
\not{g}
. Throughout this work, we shall be working with the following double null frame:

e_{A}=\partial_{\theta^{A}},\hskip 5.69054pt\text{for}\hskip 2.84526ptA=1,2,\hskip 8.53581pte_{3}:=\partial_{u}+b^{A}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\theta^{A}},\hskip 8.53581pte_{4}=\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{\underline{u}}.

(2.1)

In our case, the reason why this choice is convenient is that
\omega
, to be defined later, is zero in this frame and the torsion
\zeta
equals
-\underline{\eta}
.

In these coordinates, the metric then takes the following form:

g=-2\Omega^{2}(\text{d}u\otimes\text{d}v+\text{d}v\otimes\text{d}u)+\not{g}_{AB}(\text{d}\theta^{A}-b^{A}\text{d}u)\otimes(\text{d}\theta^{B}-b^{B}du),

(2.2)

where we impose that the shift
b
, tangent to the spheres
S_{u,v}
be vanishing at
\begin{Bmatrix}u=u_{\infty}\end{Bmatrix}\hskip 1.42262pt\cup\hskip 1.42262pt\begin{Bmatrix}\underline{u}=0\end{Bmatrix}
and that the lapse
\Omega\equiv 1
on the same set. Integration of a function
\varphi
is
S_{u,\underline{u}}
is defined as

\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\varphi\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\mu_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}=\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{\theta^{1}}\int_{\theta^{2}}\varphi\hskip 1.42262pt\tau_{i}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\theta^{1}\text{d}\theta^{2},

where
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
is a partition of unity subordinate to
D_{U_{1}},D_{U_{2}}
at
u,\underline{u}
.

2.2
Coordinates on the mass shell and the mass shell relation

Let
(p^{\mu}:\hskip 1.42262pt\mu=1,2,3,4)
be the conjugate coordinate, on each tangent space, to the double null frame just given, so that
(x^{\mu},\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\mu})
denotes the point

{\left.\kern-1.2ptp^{\mu}e_{\mu}\right|_{x}}\in T_{x}\mathcal{M}.

This then gives a frame
\begin{Bmatrix}e_{\mu},\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{p^{\mu}}\hskip 1.42262pt\mid\mu=1,2,3,4\end{Bmatrix}
on
T\mathcal{M}
. The Vlasov equation, written in this frame, then reads

p^{\mu}e_{\mu}(f)-\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{p^{\lambda}}(f)=0.

When
f
is viewed as a function on the mass shell
\mathcal{P}
, this rewrites as

p^{\mu}e_{\mu}(f)-\Gamma^{\hat{\lambda}}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{\hat{\lambda}}}(f)=0,

where
\hat{\lambda}=1,2,3
and
p^{\hat{\lambda}}
denotes the restriction of the coordinate
p^{\lambda}
to
\mathcal{P}
. Finally, the
\partial_{\overline{p}^{i}}
, for
i=1,2,3
, denote the partial derivatives with respect to this restricted coordinate system. It is easy to verify, using (
1.4
), that the following hold true:

\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}=\partial_{p^{A}}+\frac{\not{g}_{AB}p^{B}}{2p^{3}}\partial_{p^{4}},\hskip 5.69054pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}=\partial_{p^{3}}-\frac{p^{4}}{p^{3}}\partial_{p^{4}}.

(2.3)

We recall that the mass shell
P\subset T\mathcal{M}
is defined as the set of all future-pointing null vectors. Using the form of the metric given in the previous subsection together with the definition of the coordinates
p^{\mu}
and the fact that the particles are massless, we obtain the relation

\not{g}_{AB}p^{A}p^{B}-4p^{3}p^{4}=0,\hskip 8.53581pt\text{throughout the support of $f$.}

(2.4)

The above is known as the mass shell relation. In particular,
P\subset T\mathcal{M}
is a
7-
dimensional hypersurface in
T\mathcal{M}
and can be parametrised by coordinates
(u,v,\theta^{1},\theta^{2},p^{1},p^{2},p^{3})
, with
p^{4}
given by (
2.4
).

Proposition 2.1

Along a geodesic, the following holds in our choice of frame

\displaystyle\frac{p^{3}(s)}{\frac{du}{ds}}=1,

(2.5)

where
u(s):=u(\gamma(s))
.

Proof.

Making use of the fact that
\dot{u}(s):=\frac{du}{ds}=p^{3}(s)
and that, given
s\mapsto\gamma(s)
is an incoming geodesic emanating from
u=u_{\infty}
, we may use
{\left.\kern-1.2ptu\right|_{\gamma}}={\left.\kern-1.2ptu\right|_{\gamma}}(s)
as a parameter for the geodesic.The conclusion follows.

\square

Remark 2

This relation allows us to convert the inegration along the geodesic parameter
s
to the double null retarded coordinate parameter
u
i.e.,
|\int f(u(s))ds|=|\int f(s)\frac{ds}{du}du|\lesssim\int|f(u)|du
in light of the estimates verified by
p^{3}(s)
(see proposition
3.5
) since
|\Omega^{-1}-1|\lesssim\Gamma a|u|^{-2}
.

2.3
Volume form on the mass shell

Recall that the
mass shell

\mathcal{P}\subset T\mathcal{M}
is defined to be the set of all future-pointing null vectors. If a given
(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}
, then
p={\left.\kern-1.2ptp^{\mu}e_{\mu}\right|_{x}}
as a vector on
T_{x}\mathcal{M}
satisfies
g_{x}(p,p)=0
, which translates to the mass shell relation

\not{g}_{AB}p^{A}p^{B}-4p^{3}p^{4}=0.

(2.6)

To make sense of the integral in the definition of the energy-momentum tensor, we need to define a suitable volume form (Leray form) on
\mathcal{P}_{x}
over each
x
. We seek a one-form that is normal to
\mathcal{P}_{x}
. A canonical such choice is the differential of the function
\Lambda_{x}:T_{x}\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{R}
:

\Lambda_{x}(X)=g(X,X).

This is the function that measures the length of each vector in
T_{x}\mathcal{M}
. Taking the normal
-\frac{1}{2}\text{d}\Lambda_{x}
to
\mathcal{P}_{x}
, the volume form in the
(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2},p^{1},p^{2},p^{3})
system can be defined as

\frac{\sqrt{\det\not{g}}}{p^{3}}\text{d}p^{3}\wedge\text{d}p^{1}\wedge\text{d}p^{2}.

This is the unique volume form on
\mathcal{P}_{x}

compatible
with the normal
-\frac{1}{2}\text{d}\Lambda_{x}
(see also
[
27
]
). The energy momentum tensor at
x\in\mathcal{M}
thus takes the form

T_{\mu\nu}(x)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f\hskip 1.42262ptp_{\mu}\hskip 1.42262ptp_{\nu}\frac{\sqrt{\det\not{g}}}{p^{3}}\text{d}p^{1}\text{d}p^{2}\text{d}p^{3}.

2.4
Ricci coefficients and curvature components

Let
A,B
take values in
\begin{Bmatrix}1,2\end{Bmatrix}
. We define the following (Weyl) null curvature components:

\alpha_{AB}:=W(e_{A},e_{4},e_{B},e_{4}),\hskip 5.69054pt\underline{\alpha}_{AB}:=W(e_{A},e_{3},e_{B},e_{3}),

\beta_{A}:=\frac{1}{2}W(e_{A},e_{4},e_{3},e_{4}),\hskip 5.69054pt\underline{\beta}_{A}:=\frac{1}{2}W(e_{A},e_{3},e_{3},e_{4}),

\rho:=\frac{1}{4}W(e_{3},e_{4},e_{3},e_{4}),\hskip 5.69054pt\sigma=\frac{1}{4}\prescript{*}{}{W}(e_{3},e_{4},e_{3},e_{4}).

Moreover, for reasons to be explained later on, we define the
renormalized curvature components

\tilde{\beta}_{A}=\beta_{A}-\frac{1}{2}R_{A4},\hskip 5.69054pt\tilde{\underline{\beta}}_{A}=\underline{\beta}_{A}+\frac{1}{2}R_{A3}.

(2.7)

For the Ricci coefficients in this frame, we decompose as follows:

\chi_{AB}:=g(D_{A}e_{4},\hskip 1.42262pte_{B}),\hskip 5.69054pt\underline{\chi}_{AB}:=g(D_{A}e_{3},e_{B}),

\eta_{A}:=-\frac{1}{2}g(D_{3}e_{A},\hskip 1.42262pte_{4}),\hskip 5.69054pt\underline{\eta}_{A}:=-\frac{1}{2}g(D_{4}e_{A},e_{3}),

\omega:=\frac{1}{2}g(D_{4}e_{3},\hskip 1.42262pte_{4})=0,\hskip 5.69054pt\underline{\omega}:=\frac{1}{2}g(D_{3}e_{4},e_{3}),

\zeta_{A}:=\frac{1}{2}g(D_{A}e_{4},\hskip 1.42262pte_{3})=-\underline{\eta}_{A}.

Moreover, if
\gamma
denotes the induced metric on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
, we make the following further decomposition:

\chi=\hat{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\gamma,\hskip 5.69054pt\underline{\chi}=\underline{\hat{\chi}}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hskip 1.42262pt\gamma.

The Christoffel symbols of
(S_{u,v},\not{g})
with respect to
\begin{Bmatrix}e_{1},e_{2}\end{Bmatrix}
are denoted by
\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AB}:

\not{\nabla}_{e_{A}}e_{B}=\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262pte_{C}.

(2.8)

We also let
\big(\not{\Gamma}^{\circ}\big)^{C}_{AB}
denote the spherical Christoffel symbols for the Minkowski metric (on the initial incoming cone).

For the Vlasov part, define
\not{T}
to be the restriction of the energy-momentum tensor defined in equation (
1.3
) to vector fields tangent to spheres
S_{u,\underline{u}}
:

\not{T}(Y,Z)=Y^{A}Z^{B}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}f\hskip 1.42262ptp_{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp_{B},\hskip 11.38109pt\text{for}\hskip 5.69054ptY=Y^{A}e_{A},\hskip 1.42262ptZ=Z^{B}e_{B}.

(2.9)

We denote by
\not{T}_{3},\hskip 1.42262pt\not{T}_{4}
the
S_{u,\underline{u}}
1-forms defined by restricting the
1-
forms
T(e_{3},\cdot),\hskip 1.42262ptT(e_{4},\cdot)
to vector fields tangent to the spheres
S_{u,\underline{u}}
:

\not{T}_{3}(Y)=Y^{A}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}f\hskip 1.42262ptp_{3}\hskip 1.42262ptp_{A},\hskip 11.38109pt\not{T}_{4}(Y)=Y^{A}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}f\hskip 1.42262ptp_{4}\hskip 1.42262ptp_{A}.

(2.10)

Finally, we define the functions
\not{T}_{33},\hskip 1.42262pt\not{T}_{34}
and
\not{T}_{44}

by

\not{T}_{33}=\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}f\hskip 1.42262ptp_{3}\hskip 1.42262ptp_{3},\hskip 8.53581pt\not{T}_{34}=\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}f\hskip 1.42262ptp_{3}\hskip 1.42262ptp_{4},\hskip 8.53581pt\not{T}_{44}=\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}f\hskip 1.42262ptp_{4}\hskip 1.42262ptp_{4}.

(2.11)

2.5
The renormalized structure and Bianchi equations

We have the following table of Ricci coefficients (here and throughout this paper,
\nabla=D
will be used to denote the spacetime connection):

\displaystyle\nabla_{e_{A}}e_{B}=\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262pte_{C}+\frac{1}{2}\chi_{AB}\hskip 1.42262pte_{3}+\frac{1}{2}\underline{\chi}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262pte_{4},

(2.12)

\displaystyle\nabla_{e_{A}}e_{3}={\underline{\chi}_{A}}^{B}e_{B}-\underline{\eta}_{A}\hskip 1.42262pte_{3},\hskip 8.53581pt\nabla_{e_{A}}e_{4}={\chi_{A}}^{B}\hskip 1.42262pte_{B}+\underline{\eta}_{A}\hskip 1.42262pte_{4},

(2.13)

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}e_{A}=\big({\underline{\chi}_{A}}^{B}-e_{A}(b^{B})\big)\hskip 1.42262pte_{B}+\eta_{A}\hskip 1.42262pte_{3},\hskip 8.53581pt\nabla_{e_{4}}e_{A}={\chi_{A}}^{B}\hskip 1.42262pte_{B}+\underline{\eta}_{A}\hskip 1.42262pte_{4},

(2.14)

\displaystyle\nabla_{e_{3}}e_{4}=2\eta^{A}\hskip 1.42262pte_{A},\hskip 8.53581pt\nabla_{4}e_{3}=2\underline{\eta}^{B}\hskip 1.42262pte_{B},

(2.15)

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}e_{3}=\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262pte_{3},\hskip 8.53581pt\nabla_{4}e_{4}=0.

(2.16)

For the massless (and the massless only) Vlasov energy-momentum tensor, there holds
g^{\alpha\beta}T_{\alpha\beta}=0
, whence it follows that the scalar curvature vanishes and the Einstein equations reduce to
R_{\mu\nu}=T_{\mu\nu}
. Using this, the renormalized Bianchi equations take the form:

\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{3}\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha=&\not{\nabla}\hat{\otimes}\beta+4\underline{\omega}\alpha-3\left(\hat{\chi}\rho+\prescript{*}{}{\hat{\chi}}\sigma\right)+(\zeta+4\eta)\hat{\otimes}\beta\\
&-D_{4}R_{AB}+\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{B}R_{4A}+D_{A}R_{4B}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{4}R_{43}-D_{3}R_{44}\right)\not{g}_{AB},\end{split}

(2.17)

\not{\nabla}_{4}\beta+2\operatorname{tr}\chi\beta=\not{\text{div}}\alpha-2\omega\beta+\left(\eta-2\zeta\right)\cdot\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{4}R_{4A}-D_{A}R_{44}\right),

(2.18)

\not{\nabla}_{3}\beta+\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\beta=\not{\nabla}\rho+\prescript{*}{}{\not{\nabla}}\sigma+2\underline{\omega}\beta+2\hat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\beta}+3\left(\eta\rho+\prescript{*}{}{\eta}\sigma\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{A}R_{43}-D_{4}R_{3A}\right),

(2.19)

\not{\nabla}_{4}\sigma+\frac{3}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\sigma=-\not{\text{div}}\prescript{*}{}{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\hat{\chi}}\cdot\prescript{*}{}{\alpha}-(\zeta+2\underline{\eta})\cdot\prescript{*}{}{\beta}-\frac{1}{4}\left(D_{\mu}R_{4\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{4\mu}\right){\epsilon^{\mu\nu}}_{34},

(2.20)

\not{\nabla}_{3}\sigma+\frac{3}{2}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\sigma=-\not{\text{div}}\prescript{*}{}{\underline{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\hat{\chi}\cdot\prescript{*}{}{\underline{\alpha}}-(\zeta+2\eta)\cdot\prescript{*}{}{\underline{\beta}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(D_{\mu}R_{3\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{3\mu}\right){\epsilon^{\mu\nu}}_{34},

(2.21)

\not{\nabla}_{4}\rho+\frac{3}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\rho=\not{\text{div}}\beta-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\hat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha+(\zeta+2\underline{\eta})\cdot\beta-\frac{1}{4}\left(D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{43}\right),

(2.22)

\not{\nabla}_{3}\rho+\frac{3}{2}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\rho=-\not{\text{div}}\underline{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\alpha}+(\zeta-2\eta)\cdot\underline{\beta}+\frac{1}{4}\left(D_{3}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{33}\right),

(2.23)

\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\beta}+\operatorname{tr}\chi\underline{\beta}=-\not{\nabla}\rho+\prescript{*}{}{\not{\nabla}}\sigma+2\omega\underline{\beta}+2\underline{\hat{\chi}}\cdot\beta-3\left(\underline{\eta}\rho-\prescript{*}{}{\underline{\eta}}\sigma\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{A}R_{43}-D_{3}R_{4A}\right),

(2.24)

\not{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\beta}+2\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\beta}=-\not{\text{div}}\underline{\alpha}-2\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\beta}+\underline{\eta}\cdot\underline{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{A}R_{33}-D_{3}R_{3A}\right),

(2.25)

\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\alpha}=&-\not{\nabla}\hat{\otimes}\underline{\beta}+4\omega\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\alpha}-3\left(\underline{\hat{\chi}}\rho-\prescript{*}{}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}\sigma\right)+\left(\zeta-4\underline{\eta}\right)\hat{\otimes}\underline{\beta}\\
&-D_{3}R_{AB}+\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{A}R_{3B}+D_{B}R_{3A}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{3}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{43}\right)\not{g}_{AB}.\end{split}

(2.26)

We introduce several notations. For an
S_{u,\underline{u}}
1-form or a
(0,2)\hskip 1.42262ptS_{u,\underline{u}}-
tensor the left Hodge dual
∗
is defined as

\prescript{*}{}{\xi}_{A}=\not{\epsilon}_{AB}\not{g}^{BC}\xi_{C}\hskip 8.53581pt\text{and}\hskip 8.53581pt\prescript{*}{}{\xi}_{AB}=\not{\epsilon}_{AC}\not{g}^{CD}\xi_{DB},

respectively. Moreover,
\not{\epsilon}
denotes the volume form associated with the metric
\not{g}
and for a
(0,2)

S_{u,\underline{u}}
tensor
\xi
,

\not{\epsilon}\cdot\xi=\not{\epsilon}^{AB}\xi_{AB}.

The symmetric traceless product of two
S_{u,\underline{u}}
1 forms is defined by

(\xi\hat{\otimes}\xi^{\prime})_{AB}=\xi_{A}\hskip 1.42262pt\xi^{\prime}_{B}+\xi_{B}\hskip 1.42262pt\xi^{\prime}_{A}-\not{g}_{AB}(\not{g}^{CD}\hskip 1.42262pt\xi_{C}\hskip 1.42262pt\xi^{\prime}_{D}),

while the antisymmetric products are defined by

\xi\wedge\xi^{\prime}=\not{\epsilon}^{AB}\xi_{A}\xi^{\prime}_{B}\hskip 8.53581pt\text{and}\hskip 8.53581pt\xi\wedge\xi^{\prime}=\not{\epsilon}^{AB}\not{g}^{CD}\xi_{AC}\xi^{\prime}_{BD},

for two
S_{u,\underline{u}}
one-forms and
S_{u,\underline{u}}

(0,2)-
tensors respectively. Also,

(\xi\times\xi^{\prime})_{AB}=\not{g}^{CD}\xi_{AC}\xi^{\prime}_{BD},

for
S_{u,\underline{u}}

(0,2)-
tensors
\xi,\xi^{\prime}.
The symmetric trace-free derivative of an
S_{u,\underline{u}}

1-
form is defined as

(\not{\nabla}\hat{\otimes}\xi)_{AB}=\not{\nabla}_{A}\xi_{B}+\not{\nabla}_{B}\xi_{A}-(\mbox{div}\mkern-19.0mu/\,\,\,\,\xi)\not{g}_{AB}.

Finally, define the
\not{g}
-inner product of two
(0,n)

S_{u,\underline{u}}
-tensors by

(\xi,\xi^{\prime})=\not{g}^{A_{1}B_{1}}\cdot\not{g}^{A_{n}B_{n}}\xi_{A_{1}\dots A_{n}}\xi^{\prime}_{B_{1}\dots B_{n}}.

The norm of a
(0,n)

S_{u,\underline{u}}-
tensors is then given by

\lvert\xi\rvert^{2}=\not{g}^{A_{1}B_{1}}\dots\not{g}^{A_{n}B_{n}}\xi_{A_{1}\dots A_{n}}\xi_{B_{1}\dots B_{n}}.

The notation
\lvert\cdot\rvert
will also later be used, when applied to components of
S_{u,\underline{u}}
tensors, to denote the standard absolute value on
\mathbb{R}
. It will always be clear from the context which one is meant. For example, if
\xi
is an
S_{u,\underline{u}}

1-
form, then
\lvert\xi\rvert
denotes the
\not{g}
norm as above, whilst
\lvert\xi_{A}\rvert
denotes the absolute value of
\xi(e_{A})
. We now give the null structure and the metric quantities below.

\not{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\eta}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\eta}=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\eta-\underline{\hat{\chi}}\cdot(\underline{\eta}-\eta)+\underline{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\not{T}_{3},

(2.27)

\not{\nabla}_{3}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi})^{2}=-\lvert\underline{\hat{\chi}}\rvert^{2}-\not{T}_{33},

(2.28)

\not{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\hat{\chi}}+\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\hat{\chi}}=\underline{\omega}\underline{\hat{\chi}}-\underline{\alpha},

(2.29)

\not{\nabla}_{3}\big(1-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big)=\underline{\omega}-\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262pt\big(1-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big),

(2.30)

\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\omega}=2\hskip 1.42262pt\eta\cdot\underline{\eta}-\lvert\eta\rvert^{2}-\rho-\frac{1}{2}\not{T}_{34},

(2.31)

\not{\nabla}_{4}\eta+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\eta=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\eta}-\hat{\chi}\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\beta-\frac{1}{2}\not{T}_{4},

(2.32)

\not{\nabla}_{4}\operatorname{tr}\chi+\frac{1}{2}(\operatorname{tr}\chi)^{2}=-\lvert\hat{\chi}\rvert^{2}-\not{T}_{44},

(2.33)

\not{\nabla}_{4}\hat{\chi}+\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\hat{\chi}=-\alpha,

(2.34)

\not{\nabla}_{4}b=2(\underline{\eta}-\eta)+\chi\cdot b,

(2.35)

The additional propagation equations for
\underline{\hat{\chi}}
,
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}
,

\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\hat{\chi}}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\hat{\chi}}=\not{\nabla}\hat{\otimes}\underline{\eta}-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hat{\chi}+\underline{\eta}\hat{\otimes}\underline{\eta}+\hat{\not{T}},

(2.36)

\not{\nabla}_{4}\operatorname{tr}\chi=2\hskip 1.42262pt\mbox{div}\mkern-19.0mu/\,\,\,\,\underline{\eta}+2\lvert\underline{\eta}\rvert^{2}+\hat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\hat{\chi}}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\rho-\frac{1}{2}\not{T}_{34},

(2.37)

will also be used later in Section
8
to derive a propagation equation for the (renormalized) aspect function
\mu
. Here
\hat{\not{T}}=\not{T}-\not{T}_{34}\not{g}
is the trace-free part of
\not{T}
. The following first variational formula for the induced metric on the spheres will also be used,

\mathcal{L}_{e_{3}}\not{g}=2\underline{\chi},

(2.38)

\mathcal{L}_{e_{4}}\not{g}=2\chi,

(2.39)

where
\mathcal{L}
denotes the Lie derivative.

An equation for
\Gamma^{A}_{3B}

Adjoint to the above equations, the equation for
\Gamma^{A}_{3B}={\underline{\chi}_{A}}^{B}-e_{A}(b^{B})
will be pivotal to our analysis. We derive it below. First of all, there holds

\displaystyle\Gamma^{A}_{3B}=\not{g}^{AC}\langle\nabla_{3}e_{B},e_{C}\rangle.

(2.40)

Therefore,

\nabla_{4}\Gamma^{A}_{3B}=\langle\nabla_{3}e_{B},e_{C}\rangle\hskip 1.42262pt\nabla_{4}(\not{g}^{AC})+\not{g}^{AC}(\langle\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}e_{B},e_{C})+\langle\nabla_{3}e_{B},\nabla_{4}e_{C}\rangle).

(2.41)

We have

\not{g}^{AC}\langle\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}e_{B},e_{C}\rangle=\not{g}^{AC}\big(\langle\nabla_{3}\nabla_{4}e_{B},e_{C}\rangle+R_{43BC}+\langle\nabla_{[e_{4},e_{3}]}e_{B},e_{C}\rangle\big).

However,

•

The third term

[e_{4},e_{3}]=2(\underline{\eta}^{D}-\eta^{D})e_{D}+\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262pte_{4},

hence

\not{g}^{AC}\langle\nabla_{[e_{4},e_{3}]}e_{B},e_{C}\rangle=\not{g}^{AC}\big(2(\underline{\eta}^{D}-\eta^{D})\hskip 1.42262pt\not{\Gamma}^{E}_{DB}\hskip 1.42262pt\not{g}_{EC}+\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262pt\chi_{BC}\big)=2(\underline{\eta}^{D}-\eta^{D})\hskip 1.42262pt\not{\Gamma}^{A}_{DB}+\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262pt{\chi_{B}}^{A},

(2.42)

•

The second term
\not{g}^{AC}R_{43BC}={R^{A}}_{B34}
,

•

The first term

\displaystyle\not{g}^{AC}\langle\nabla_{3}\nabla_{4}e_{B},e_{C}\rangle=

\displaystyle\not{g}^{AC}\langle\nabla_{3}({\chi_{B}}^{D}e_{D}+\underline{\eta}_{B}e_{4}),e_{C}\rangle=\not{g}^{AC}(\not{g}_{DC}\nabla_{3}{\chi_{B}}^{D}+{\chi_{B}}^{D}\not{\Gamma}^{E}_{3D}\not{g}_{EC}+\underline{\eta}_{B}\eta^{D}\not{g}_{DC})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}{\chi_{B}}^{A}+{\chi_{B}}^{D}\not{\Gamma}^{A}_{3D}+\underline{\eta}_{B}\eta^{A}.

(2.43)

For the term
\not{g}^{AC}\langle\nabla_{3}e_{B},\nabla_{4}e_{C}\rangle
a straightforward computation gives

\not{g}^{AC}\hskip 1.42262pt\langle\nabla_{3}e_{B},\nabla_{4}e_{C}\rangle={\chi_{D}}^{A}\hskip 1.42262pt\not{\Gamma}^{D}_{3B}-2\hskip 1.42262pt\eta_{B}\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\eta}^{A}

(2.44)

Putting everything together, we get the important equation

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\Gamma^{A}_{3B}=-{\chi_{D}}^{A}\Gamma^{D}_{3B}+\nabla_{3}{\chi_{B}}^{A}+{\chi_{B}}^{D}\Gamma^{A}_{3D}+\underline{\eta}_{B}\eta^{A}+{R^{A}}_{B34}+2(\underline{\eta}^{D}-\eta^{D})\not{\Gamma}^{A}_{DB}+\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262pt{\chi_{B}}^{A}-2\eta_{B}\underline{\eta}^{A}.

(2.45)

Through it, one can avoid having to obtain elliptic estimates for most of the Ricci coefficients as well as for
b
itself. Indeed, the crucial observation is that
\nabla_{3}\chi
contains only
\not{\nabla}\eta
in it.

2.6
Integration and
L^{p}
Norms

Let
(\mathcal{M},g)
be a smooth Lorentzian manifold foliated by a double null gauge, with null coordinates
(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
and associated null hypersurfaces

H_{u}:=\{(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2}):\underline{u}\geq 0\},\qquad\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}:=\{(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2}):u\geq u_{\infty}\}.

For each pair
(u,\underline{u})
, we denote by
S_{u,\underline{u}}
the spacelike
2
–sphere of intersection

S_{u,\underline{u}}:=H_{u}\cap\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}.

The induced Riemannian metric on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
is denoted by
\gamma=\not{g}_{AB}\,d\theta^{A}\otimes d\theta^{B}
, with determinant
\det\not{g}
.

Fix a smooth coordinate chart
U\subset S_{u,\underline{u}}
and a smooth partition of unity
\{p_{U}\}
subordinate to such charts. For a measurable scalar function
\phi:S_{u,\underline{u}}\to\mathbb{R}
we define the surface integral by

\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\phi:=\sum_{U}\int_{U}\phi(\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\,p_{U}(\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\,\sqrt{\det\not{g}(\theta)}\,d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2},

(2.46)

which is independent of the chosen partition.
The induced metric on the null hypersurface is degenerate. We define the volume form on
H_{u}
induced by
(g,\gamma)
as follows

d\mu_{H_{u}}=2\Omega\,\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,d\underline{u}\,d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2},

where
\Omega
denotes the null lapse. For
\phi:H_{u}\to\mathbb{R}
, we set

\int_{H_{u}}\phi:=\sum_{U}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{U}\phi(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta)\,p_{U}(\theta)\,2\Omega(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta)\,\sqrt{\det\not{g}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta)}\,d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2}\,d\underline{u}^{\prime}.

(2.47)

Similarly, on the incoming null hypersurface
\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)}:=\{\underline{u}=\mathrm{const},\,u_{\infty}\leq u^{\prime}\leq u\}
, we define the volume form as

d\mu_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}=2\Omega\,\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,du\,d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2},

and we define

\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)}}\phi:=\sum_{U}\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{U}\phi(u^{\prime},\underline{u},\theta)\,p_{U}(\theta)\,2\Omega(u^{\prime},\underline{u},\theta)\,\sqrt{\det\not{g}(u^{\prime},\underline{u},\theta)}\,d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2}\,du^{\prime}.

(2.48)

For the spacetime slab

\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}:=\{(u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\mid u_{\infty}\leq u^{\prime}\leq u,\ 0\leq\underline{u}^{\prime}\leq\underline{u}\},

the natural
4
–volume form induced by
g
in double null coordinates is

d\mu_{g}=\Omega^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,du\,d\underline{u}\,d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2}.

Accordingly, for
\phi:\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}\to\mathbb{R}
, we set

\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\phi:=\sum_{U}\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{U}\phi(u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta)\,p_{U}(\theta)\,\Omega^{2}(u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta)\,\sqrt{\det\not{g}(u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta)}\,d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2}\,d\underline{u}^{\prime}\,du^{\prime}.

(2.49)

Let
\phi
be a tensor field tangent to
S_{u,\underline{u}}
, and let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\not}{g}
denote the pointwise inner product induced by
\not{g}
. For
1\leq p<\infty
we define

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{p}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}^{p}

\displaystyle:=\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\phi,\phi\rangle_{\not}{g}^{\frac{p}{2}},

(2.50)

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{p}(H_{u})}^{p}

\displaystyle:=\int_{H_{u}}\langle\phi,\phi\rangle_{\not}{g}^{\frac{p}{2}},

(2.51)

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{p}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}})}^{p}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}\langle\phi,\phi\rangle_{\not}{g}^{\frac{p}{2}}.

(2.52)

For the case
p=\infty
, we set

\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}:=\sup_{(\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\in S_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\phi,\phi\rangle_{\not}{g}^{1/2}(\theta^{1},\theta^{2}).

(2.53)

All norms are understood with respect to the appropriate induced measures defined above.

2.7
Signature for decay rates and scale-invariant norms

Perhaps the most challenging aspect of trapped surface formation results, historically, has been the attempt to find initial data that are, in an appropriate sense, large (this is by necessity, as is implied by the monumental work of
[
15
]
) but also small enough to allow for an existence result of a spacetime region that gives trapped surfaces the time they would require to form. The first such initial data set, in the absence of symmetries, was given by
[
13
]
. Later contributions include
[
19
]
,
[
6
]
and
[
2
]
. Moreover, one would have to construct norms which preserve, at least approximately, the hierarchy present in the initial data upon evolution of the Einstein equations. The signature for decay rates, which was first introduced in
[
3
]
, is the tool we will use in the present paper to build
scale-invariant norms
. These will be norms that, upon evolution of the initial data, remain bounded above by a uniform constant (with the exception of a few anomalous terms). For another application of this framework, see
[
4
]
.

To each
\phi\in\begin{Bmatrix}\alpha,\underline{\alpha},\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\underline{\beta}},\rho,\sigma,\eta,\underline{\eta},\chi,\underline{\chi},\omega,\underline{\omega},\zeta,\gamma\end{Bmatrix}
we associate its
signature for decay rates

s_{2}(\phi)
:

s_{2}(\phi)=0\cdot N_{4}(\phi)+\frac{1}{2}N_{A}(\phi)+1\cdot N_{3}(\phi)-1.

Here
N_{\alpha}(\phi)

(\alpha=1,2,3,4)
denotes the number of times
e_{\alpha}
appears in the definition of
\phi
. We also extend the definition of
s_{2}
in the same way to the set of energy-momentum tensor components. For the Ricci coefficients and curvature components, we get the following table of signatures.

\phi

\alpha

\underline{\alpha}

\beta

\underline{\beta}

\rho

\sigma

\eta

\underline{\eta}

\chi

\underline{\chi}

\underline{\omega}

\zeta

\not{g}

\lvert u\rvert

s_{2}(\phi)

0

2

0.5

1.5

1

1

0.5

0.5

0

1

1

0.5

0

-1

Accordingly, for the matter components, we get the following table of signatures:

T_{\mu\nu}

\not{T}_{44}

\not{T}_{34}

\not{T}_{33}

\not{T}_{3}

\not{T}_{4}

\not{T}

s_{2}(T_{\mu\nu})

0

1

2

\frac{3}{2}

\frac{1}{2}

1

Several properties of
s_{2}
follow:

s_{2}(\not{\nabla}_{4}\phi)=s_{2}(\phi),

s_{2}(\not{\nabla}\phi)=s_{2}(\phi)+\frac{1}{2}\hskip 5.69054pts_{2}(\not{\nabla}_{3}\phi)=s_{2}(\phi)+1.

Finally, perhaps the most important property of
s_{2}
is
signature conservation
:

s_{2}(\phi_{1}\cdot\phi_{2})=s_{2}(\phi_{1})+s_{2}(\phi_{2}),\hskip 5.69054pt

(2.54)

This allows for the (almost)-preservation of the scale-invariant norms upon evolution, as we shall see.

An aside: Assigning signature to
\lvert u\rvert,\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}

In the paper
[
1
]
that introduced the current scale-invariant framework, the only type of derivative that required control at the level of Bianchi or structure equations was that of an angular derivative
\not{\nabla}
. Indeed, in vacuum, there exists no extra inhomogeneity in the Bianchi equations stemming from matter error terms. Therefore, to obtain control on the Ricci coefficients up to top order (and hence to obtain energy control of the curvature components), it was enough to only use angular derivatives
4
4

4

Note that, strictly speaking, the spacetime existence theorem only requires control on angular derivatives of the curvature components. However, in non-vacuum models and in particular the Einstein-Yang-Mills system, for example, obtaining such control and closing the energy arguments requires control on
\not{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\alpha}_{F},\not{\nabla}_{4}\alpha_{F}
, where
\underline{\alpha}_{F}
and
\alpha_{F}
correspond to the Yang-Mills source term (see
[
8
]
).
. In our current setting, obtaining control on two derivatives of curvature requires control on the Ricci coefficients up to second order (up to third order for
\eta,\hat{\chi},\operatorname{tr}\chi
), which in turn (through the use of the structure equations) necessitates control on the matter terms and ultimately on
\mathcal{D}^{i}T_{\mu\nu}
, where
\mathcal{D}=\{\lvert u\rvert\not{\nabla}_{3},\not{\nabla}_{4},a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}\}
and
i=0,\dots,3
. Therefore,
\lvert u\rvert
appears as part of error terms for various equations and it is useful to assign a signature to it. Because

\not{\nabla}_{3}\lvert u\rvert=-1,

the function
\lvert u\rvert
inherits a signature
s_{2}(\lvert u\rvert)=-1
. Similar considerations
5
5

5

Interestingly, the term
\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}
does not appear as part of any error terms, but that is only because of the frame choice. In the frame
e_{A}=\partial_{\theta^{A}},e_{3}:=\partial_{u}+b^{A}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\theta^{A}},\hskip 8.53581pte_{4}=\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{v}
, say, terms like
\frac{\Omega^{-1}-1}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}
would come up (this particular one would appear in the structure equation for
\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}
, for example). This is another simplification arising from our choice of frame.
imply
s_{2}\big(\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}\big)=1
.

For any horizontal tensor-field or stress-energy-momentum tensor component
\phi
, we define the following norms:

\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}:=a^{-s_{2}(\phi)}\lvert u\rvert^{2s_{2}(\phi)+1}\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(2.55)

\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}:=a^{-s_{2}(\phi)}\lvert u\rvert^{2s_{2}(\phi)}\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(2.56)

and more generally

\displaystyle||\varphi||_{L^{p}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}=\frac{a^{s_{2}(\varphi})}{|u|^{2s_{2}(\varphi)+\frac{p-2}{p}}}||\varphi||_{L^{p}_{sc}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},~~1\leq p<\infty

(2.57)

Notice the difference in the
u
-weights amongst the definitions.

A crucial property of the above norms is the
scale-invariant Hölder’s inequalities
that they satisfy. For
\mathrm{\Upsilon}
denoting an arbitrary
S-
tensor field, the following hold:

||\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{2}||_{L^{1}_{sc}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{1}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lVert{\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{2}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(2.58)

||\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{2}||_{L^{1}_{sc}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{1}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}||\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{2}||_{L^{1}_{sc}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(2.59)

\lVert{\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{2}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{1}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lVert{\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{2}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(2.60)

and

||\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{2}||_{L^{2}_{sc}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}||\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{1}||_{L^{4}_{sc}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}||\mathrm{\Upsilon}_{2}||_{L^{4}_{sc}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

(2.61)

Notice that this is possible partly thanks to the signature conservation property (
2.54
). In the region of study, the factor
\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}
plays the role that
\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}
plays in the definition of the corresponding norms
6
6

6

See Section 2.17 in
[
19
]
.
in [Kl-Rod], namely that of measuring the
smallness
of the nonlinear terms. The above inequalities are the primary tools that will be used to close the bootstrap argument required for the existence part.

2.8
Norms

Following An’s
[
3
]
, we define
\prescript{(S)}{}{\mathcal{O}_{s,p}}
norms on a given
S_{u,\underline{u}}
.

\displaystyle\prescript{(S)}{}{\mathcal{O}_{0,\infty}}(u,\underline{u}):=

\displaystyle\lVert{\eta,\underline{\eta},\omega}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\hat{\chi}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\lVert{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(2.62)

\displaystyle\prescript{(S)}{}{\mathcal{O}_{0,4}}(u,\underline{u}):=

\displaystyle\lVert{\eta,\underline{\eta},\omega}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\hat{\chi}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\lVert{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(2.63)

\displaystyle\prescript{(S)}{}{\mathcal{O}_{1,4}}(u,\underline{u}):=\sum_{\mathcal{D}\in\{\lvert u\rvert\not{\nabla}_{3},\not{\nabla}_{4},a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}\}}

\displaystyle\lVert{\mathcal{D}(\eta,\underline{\eta},\omega)}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}\hat{\chi}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\mathcal{D}\underline{\hat{\chi}}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(2.64)

\displaystyle\prescript{(S)}{}{\mathcal{O}_{2,2}}(u,\underline{u}):=\sum_{\mathcal{D}\in\{\lvert u\rvert\not{\nabla}_{3},\not{\nabla}_{4},a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}\}}

\displaystyle\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}(\eta,\underline{\eta},\underline{\omega})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\hat{\chi}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\underline{\hat{\chi}}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

(2.65)

We also define the elliptic norm

\displaystyle\prescript{(S)}{}{\mathcal{O}_{3,2}}(u,\underline{u}):=\sum_{\mathcal{D}\in\{\lvert u\rvert\not{\nabla}_{3},\not{\nabla}_{4},a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}\}}

\displaystyle\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\not{\nabla}(\eta,\operatorname{tr}\chi,\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\hat{\chi})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})}.

(2.66)

We denote by
\mathcal{O}_{0,\infty},\mathcal{O}_{0,4},\mathcal{O}_{1,4},\mathcal{O}_{2,4},\mathcal{O}_{3,2}
the suprema of the corresponding norms, respectively, over all values
(u,\underline{u})
, where
u_{\infty}\leq u\leq-a/4
and
0\leq\underline{u}\leq 1
. Finally, we define the total Ricci norm

\mathcal{O}:=\mathcal{O}_{0,\infty}+\mathcal{O}_{0,4}+\mathcal{O}_{1,4}+\mathcal{O}_{2,2}+\mathcal{O}_{3,2},

while by
\mathcal{O}^{(0)}
we denote the total norm on the initial hypersurface
H_{u_{\infty}}
. Moreover, we introduce the following norms on curvature that will be estimated.

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{0}=\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\alpha}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})}+\lVert{(\tilde{\beta},\rho,\sigma,\tilde{\underline{\beta}})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})},

(2.67)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}=\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\tilde{\beta}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}+\lVert{(\rho,\sigma,\tilde{\underline{\beta}},\underline{\alpha})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})},

(2.68)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}:=\sum_{\mathcal{D}\in\{\lvert u\rvert\not{\nabla}_{3},\not{\nabla}_{4},a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}\}}\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}\alpha}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})}+\lVert{\mathcal{D}(\tilde{\beta},\rho,\sigma,\tilde{\underline{\beta}})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})},

(2.69)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}=\sum_{\mathcal{D}\in\{\lvert u\rvert\not{\nabla}_{3},\not{\nabla}_{4},a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}\}}\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}\tilde{\beta}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}+\lVert{\mathcal{D}(\rho,\sigma,\tilde{\underline{\beta}},\underline{\alpha})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})},

(2.70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}:=\sum_{\mathcal{D}\in\{\lvert u\rvert\not{\nabla}_{3},\not{\nabla}_{4},a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}\}}\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\alpha}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})}+\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}(\tilde{\beta},\rho,\sigma,\tilde{\underline{\beta}})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})},

(2.71)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}=\sum_{\mathcal{D}\in\{\lvert u\rvert\not{\nabla}_{3},\not{\nabla}_{4},a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}\}}\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\tilde{\beta}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}+\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}(\rho,\sigma,\tilde{\underline{\beta}},\underline{\alpha})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})},

(2.72)

We set
\mathcal{R}_{0},\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}
to be the supremum over all
(u,\underline{u})
in the spacetime slab of
\mathcal{R}_{0}(u,\underline{u}),\mathcal{R}_{1}(u,\underline{u})
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}(u,\underline{u})
respectively. Similarly, we define
\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0},\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
and
\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
. We define the total curvature norms

\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}_{0}+\mathcal{R}_{1}+\mathcal{R}_{2},\hskip 8.53581pt\underline{\mathcal{R}}=\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}.

(2.73)

Finally, we define
\mathcal{R}^{(0)}
to be the initial value of the norm:

\mathcal{R}^{(0)}:=\sup_{0\leq u\leq 1}\big(\mathcal{R}_{0}(u_{\infty},u)+\mathcal{R}_{1}(u_{\infty},u)+\mathcal{R}_{2}(u_{\infty},u)\big).

(2.74)

For the Vlasov norm, we define, similarly, the following quantities:

\mathcal{V}_{0}=\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}{a}\lvert\not{T}_{44}\rvert+\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{4}}{a}\lvert\not{T}_{43}\rvert+\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{6}}{a}\lvert\not{T}_{33}\rvert+\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{5}}{a}\lvert\not{T}_{5}\rvert+\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{3}}{a}\lvert\not{T}_{3}\rvert+\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{4}}{a}\lvert\not{T}\rvert.

(2.75)

For
j=1,2,3,
let us first introduce the following vector fields:

\displaystyle V_{(A)}:=Hor(e_{A})-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}},\hskip 1.42262ptV_{(3)}:=Hor(e_{4}),\hskip 8.53581ptV_{(4)}:=p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}-\lvert u\rvert Hor(e_{3}),\hskip 8.53581ptV_{(4+A)}:=\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}},

(2.76)

together with the vector field

V_{(0)}:=\lvert u\rvert Hor(e_{3}).

(2.77)

We define the set

\widetilde{V}:=\{\lvert u\rvert V_{0},\lvert u\rvert V_{(4+A)},V_{(A)},V_{(3)},V_{(4)}\}.

(2.78)

We introduce the Vlasov norms

\mathfrak{V}_{1}(u,\underline{u}):=\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}{a^{2}}\sum_{\widetilde{V}_{(j)}\in\widetilde{V}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\lvert\widetilde{V}_{(j)}f\rvert^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{\text{d}p^{1}\text{d}p^{2}\text{d}p^{3}}{p^{3}},

(2.79)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}_{2}(u,\underline{u}):=\frac{1}{a^{2}}\sum_{V_{(1)},V_{(2)}\in\tilde{V}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|\widetilde{V}_{(1)}\widetilde{V}_{(2)}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\hskip 1.42262ptd^{2}\theta

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}_{3}(u):=\frac{1}{a^{2}}\sum_{\tilde{V}_{(1)},\tilde{V}_{(2)},\tilde{V}_{(3)}\in\tilde{V}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|\tilde{V}_{(1)}\tilde{V}_{(2)}\tilde{V}_{(3)}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\hskip 1.42262ptd^{2}\theta\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}.

We let
\mathcal{V}_{(i)}
, for
i=0
to
3
, denote the suprema of the corresponding-order Vlasov norms respectively over
(u,\underline{u})
in the spacetime slab and define the total norm

\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}_{0}+\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}+\mathcal{V}_{3}.

We proceed to make the fundamental bootstrap assumptions:

\mathcal{O}\leq O,\hskip 8.53581pt\mathcal{R}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}\leq R,\hskip 8.53581pt\mathcal{V}\leq V,

(2.80)

where
7
7

7

Let us note that throughout the text, we might also use
\Gamma
as a symbol for
O

O,R,V
are large constants such that, however,
(O+R+V)^{20}\ll a^{\frac{1}{16}}.

3
Preliminary estimates

3.1
Preliminary bootstrap assumptions

We will be employing a bootstrap argument to obtain a priori bounds on
\Gamma
,
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{V}
. Along the initial hypersurfaces
H_{u_{\infty}}
and
\underline{H}_{0}
, an analysis of the initial data using transport equations (see for example,
[
21
]
) yields

\Gamma_{0}+\mathcal{R}_{0}+\mathcal{V}_{0}\lesssim\mathcal{I}.

Our goal is to show that in the entire region

\mathcal{D}:=\begin{Bmatrix}(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\hskip 2.84526pt\mid\hskip 2.84526ptu_{\infty}\leq u\leq-\frac{a}{4},\hskip 1.42262pt0\leq\underline{u}\leq 1\end{Bmatrix}

there exists a constant
c(\mathcal{I})=\mathcal{I}^{4}+\mathcal{I}^{2}+\mathcal{I}+1
such that

\Gamma+\mathcal{R}+\mathcal{V}\lesssim c(\mathcal{I}).

We assume, as a bootstrap assumption the following:

\Gamma\leq\Gamma,\hskip 5.69054pt\mathcal{R}\leq R,\hskip 5.69054pt\mathcal{V}\leq M,

(3.1)

where
\Gamma,\hskip 1.42262ptR
and
M
are large so that

\mathcal{I}^{4}+\mathcal{I}^{2}+\mathcal{I}+1\ll\text{min}\begin{Bmatrix}\Gamma,\hskip 1.42262ptR,\hskip 1.42262ptM\end{Bmatrix}

but also such that

(\Gamma+R+M)^{20}\leq a^{\frac{1}{16}}.

We first show that
\Omega
is always close to 1 in the supremum norm.

Proposition 3.1

There holds

\big\lVert 1-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{a\hskip 1.42262ptO^{2}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}

everywhere in the slab of existence.

Proof.

Using the auxiliary bootstrap assumption
\big\lVert 1-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq O,
we use the equation

\not{\nabla}_{3}\big(1-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big)=\underline{\omega}-\underline{\omega}\big(1-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big),

(3.2)

we obtain

\big\lVert 1-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\lVert\underline{\omega}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}})}(1+O)\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}u^{\prime}\leq\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\frac{a\hskip 1.42262ptO}{\lvert u^{\prime}\rvert^{3}}(1+O)\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}u^{\prime}\leq\frac{a\hskip 1.42262ptO^{2}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}.

(3.3)

Here we have made use of the bootstrap assumption
\lVert{\underline{\omega}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}=a^{-1}\lvert u\rvert^{3}\lVert{\underline{\omega}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq O
.

\square

We proceed to obtain control on the induced metric
\not{g}
on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
.

Proposition 3.2

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
), for the induced metric
\not{g}
on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
we have

c^{\prime}\leq\frac{\det\not{g}(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})}{\lvert u\rvert^{4}}\leq C^{\prime}.

Here
c^{\prime}
and
C^{\prime}

are constants depending only on the initial data. Moreover, throughout the slab of existence there holds

\lvert\not{g}_{AB}(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\rvert\leq C^{\prime}\lvert u\rvert^{2},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\not{g}^{AB}(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\rvert\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}.

Proof.

We argue through the first variational formula
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{e_{4}}\not{g}_{AB}=2\hat{\chi}_{AB}
. This implies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\underline{u}}\log(\det\not{g})=2\operatorname{tr}\chi.

(3.4)

Let
\not{g}_{0}(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})=\not{g}(u,0,\theta^{1},\theta^{2}).
Then with
\lvert 2\operatorname{tr}\chi\rvert\leq O/\lvert u\rvert
, it follows

\frac{\det\not{g}}{\det\not{g}_{0}}=\mathrm{e}^{\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}2\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\frac{O}{a}}.

By Taylor expansion, this implies

\lvert\det\not{g}-\det\not{g}_{0}\rvert\leq\det\not{g}_{0}\lvert 1-\mathrm{e}^{\frac{O}{a}}\rvert\lesssim\frac{O}{a}\det\not{g}_{0},

which gives the upper and lower bounds for
\det\not{g}
. For
\not{g}
, assume a bootstrap assumption of the form

\lvert\not{g}_{AB}\rvert\leq\lvert u\rvert^{2}G,

where
G
is a large constant with
G\ll a
.
let
\Lambda
denote the greater eigenvalue. We have

\Lambda\leq\sup_{A,B=1,2}\not{g}_{AB}\leq\lvert u\rvert^{2}G,

\sum_{A,B=1,2}\lvert\chi_{AB}\rvert\leq\Lambda\lVert{\chi}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

\lvert\not{g}_{AB}-(\not{g}_{0})_{AB}\rvert\leq\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lvert\chi_{AB}\rvert\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}\leq\Lambda\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert}O\leq\lvert u\rvert^{2}\frac{O\hskip 1.42262ptG}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\lvert u\rvert^{2}.

Given the value of
(\not{g}_{0})_{AB}
, this improves the bootstrap assumption and proves the result. We further bound
\not{g}^{AB}
from above by using the upper bound for
\lvert\gamma_{AB}\rvert
and the lower bound for
\det\not{g}
.

\square

For the metric
\not{g}
, we will also need the following:

Proposition 3.3

We continue to work under the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
). Fix a point
(u,\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
on the initial hypersurface
\underline{H}_{0}
. Along the outgoing null geodesics emitting from
(u,\theta^{1},\theta^{2}),
denote by
\Lambda(\underline{u})
and
\lambda(\underline{u})
the larger and smaller eigenvalues of
\not{g}^{-1}(u,\underline{u}=0,\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\not{g}(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
respectively. Then we have

\lvert\Lambda(\underline{u})-1\rvert+\lvert\lambda(\underline{u})-1\rvert\leq\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Proof.

Define
\nu(\underline{u}):=\sqrt{\frac{\Lambda(\underline{u})}{\lambda(\underline{u})}}
. Following
[
1
]
or the derivation of
(5.93)
in
[
13
]
, by the first variational formula we can derive

\nu(\underline{u})\leq 1+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lvert\hat{\chi}(\underline{u}^{\prime})\rvert_{\not{g}}\hskip 1.42262pt\nu(\underline{u}^{\prime})\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}.

By Grönwall’s inequality, this implies

\lvert\nu(\underline{u})\rvert\lesssim 1\hskip 8.53581pt\text{and}\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\nu(\underline{u})-1\rvert\leq\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.42262ptO}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{a}.

The desired estimate then follows.

\square

The two Propositions above also imply control on the area of the spheres
S_{u,\underline{u}}
:

Proposition 3.4

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
), there holds

\sup_{\underline{u}}\lvert\text{Area}(S_{u,\underline{u}})-\text{Area}(S_{u,0})\rvert\leq\frac{O^{\frac{1}{2}}}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lvert u\rvert^{2}.

Proof.

The result follows from the fact that

\text{Area}(S_{u,\underline{u}})=\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\theta^{1}\text{d}\theta^{2},

together with the bounds on
\det\not{g}
obtained in Proposition
3.2
.

\square

Regarding the Vlasov matter, we finish this section by showing the bounds that our initial data assumptions in Theorem
1.2
impose on the support of
f
.

3.2
Decay for the momentum vector components and the support of
f

In this section, we discuss the behaviour of the various energy-momentum tensor components for the Vlasov matter (in the zeroth order). To this end, we establish a control on the size of the support of
f
.

Throughout this section,
\mathfrak{\gamma}
will denote a null geodesic emanating from
\begin{Bmatrix}u=u_{\infty}\end{Bmatrix}
, meaning
u(\gamma(0))=u_{\infty}
. Assume that
(\gamma(0),\hskip 1.42262pt\dot{\gamma}(0))\in\hskip 1.42262pt\text{supp}(f).
The tangent vector to
\gamma
at time
s
is then written

\dot{\gamma}(s)=\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}(s)\hskip 1.42262pte_{A}+p^{3}(s)\hskip 1.42262pte_{3}+p^{4}(s)\hskip 1.42262pte_{4}.

(3.5)

By assumption, we note that
(\gamma(0),\dot{\gamma}(0))\in\hskip 1.42262pt\text{supp}(f)
implies that there exist constants
C_{p^{A}},\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{3}},\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}>0
, independent of
u_{\infty}
(here A=1, 2), such that
\text{max}\begin{Bmatrix}C_{p^{A}},\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{3}},\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\end{Bmatrix}\ll a
and

0<p^{3}(0)\leq C_{p^{3}},\hskip 1.42262pt0\leq\lvert u_{\infty}\rvert^{2}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}(0)\leq C_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262pt\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0),

(3.6)

\lvert u_{\infty}\rvert^{2}\lvert p^{A}(0)\rvert\leq C_{p^{A}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0).

(3.7)

The main result of this section is a propagation-of-decay statement for the momentum components. This is the content of the following Proposition.

Proposition 3.5

Along such a geodesic
\gamma
, given the initial conditions (
3.6
)-(
3.7
), the following must hold:

\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0)\leq\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(s)\leq\frac{3}{2}p^{3}(0),\hskip 8.53581pt0\leq\lvert u(s)\rvert^{2}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}(s)\leq\frac{3}{2}\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0),

\lvert u(s)\rvert^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\lvert p^{A}(s)\rvert\leq\frac{3}{2}\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{A}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0),

for
A=1,\hskip 1.42262pt2
and for all
s
such that
\gamma(s)
lies in the slab of existence
\mathcal{D}
.

Proof.

The evolution of the various momentum components along the geodesic is given by the
geodesic equations
:

\dot{p}^{\mu}(s)+\Gamma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}(s)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\alpha}(s)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\beta}(s)=0.

In the frame constructed, they read as follows:

\begin{split}\dot{p}^{4}(s)=-\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hskip 1.42262pt\not{g}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}(s)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}(s)-\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}(s)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}(s)-2\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\eta}_{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}(s)p^{4}(s),\end{split}

(3.8)

\begin{split}\dot{p}^{3}(s)=-\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\not{g}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}(s)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}(s)-\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\hat{\chi}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}(s)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}(s)-(\eta_{A}-\underline{\eta}_{A})\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}(s)p^{3}(s)-\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(s)p^{3}(s),\end{split}

(3.9)

\begin{split}\dot{p}^{A}(s)=&-\not{\Gamma}^{A}_{BC}p^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{C}-\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}-\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}-2\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\hat{\chi}}^{A}_{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}-2\hskip 1.42262pt\hat{\chi}^{A}_{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}+(\not{\nabla}_{e_{B}}b)^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}\\
&-b^{C}\hskip 1.42262pt\hskip 1.42262pt\not{\Gamma}^{A}_{BC}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}-2\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}\hskip 1.42262pt(\eta_{A}+\underline{\eta}_{A}).\end{split}

(3.10)

At this point, we note the control on the various Ricci coefficients and the metric coefficient
\gamma
stemming from the hierarchy:

\begin{split}\lvert\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\rvert\lesssim&\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\underline{\hat{\chi}}\rvert\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\underline{\eta}\rvert\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\underline{\omega}\rvert\lesssim\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert^{3}},\\
&\lvert\operatorname{tr}\chi\rvert\lesssim\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\hat{\chi}\rvert\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\eta\rvert\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},\\
&\lVert{\not{\Gamma}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},\hskip 1.42262pt\lVert{b}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}.\end{split}

(3.11)

We use the following bootstrap assumptions:

\frac{1}{4}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0)\leq\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(s)\leq 2p^{3}(0),\hskip 8.53581pt0\leq\lvert u(s)\rvert^{2}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}(s)\leq 2\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0),

(3.12)

\lvert u(s)\rvert^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\lvert p^{A}(s)\rvert\leq 2\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{A}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0),

(3.13)

for
A=1,\hskip 1.42262pt2
and for all
s
such that
\gamma(s)
lies in the slab of existence
\mathcal{D}
.

We begin with
p^{3}
. Given the control above, we arrive at

\lvert\dot{p}^{3}(s)\rvert\leq\frac{4\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\left(p^{3}(0)\right)^{2}}{\lvert u\rvert^{3}}

(3.14)

Since the geodesic is incoming, one could use the restricted function
{\left.\kern-1.2ptu\right|_{\gamma}}
as a different parameter for the geodesic. The relation between
u
and
s
is given by

\frac{\text{d}u}{\text{d}s}=p^{3}(s)

(3.15)

This implies that

\frac{\text{d}p^{3}}{\text{d}u}=\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{1}{p^{3}}\hskip 1.42262pt\dot{p}^{3}.

(3.16)

We thus arrive at

\big\lvert\frac{\text{d}p^{3}}{\text{d}u}\big\rvert=\big\lvert\frac{\dot{p}^{3}}{p^{3}}\big\rvert\leq\frac{4\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\left(p^{3}(0)\right)^{2}}{\frac{1}{4}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0)\hskip 1.42262pt\lvert u\rvert^{3}}\leq\frac{16\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0)}{\lvert u\rvert^{3}}.

(3.17)

Integrating along the
e_{3}
direction, we thus obtain

\lvert p^{3}(u)-p^{3}(u_{\infty})\rvert\leq\frac{16\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty})}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},

(3.18)

whence we arrive at

\left(1-\frac{16\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\right)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty})\leq p^{3}(u)\hskip 1.42262pt\leq\left(1+\frac{16\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\right)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty}),

(3.19)

which implies the desired control on
p^{3}
. For
p^{4},
have

\begin{split}\frac{d(\lvert u\rvert^{2}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4})}{du}&=\lvert u\rvert^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{\dot{p}^{4}}{p^{3}}-2\lvert u\rvert\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}=\lvert u\rvert^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\left(-(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert})\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}-\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}}{p^{3}}-\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{2\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\eta}_{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}}{p^{3}}-\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}\right).\end{split}

(3.20)

Using the bound
\big\lvert\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}\big\rvert\leq\frac{O}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}
, where
O
is the bootstrap constant for the total Ricci norm (TBD), we have the following:

\big\lvert\left(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}\right)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}\big\rvert\leq\frac{2\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty})}{\lvert u\rvert^{4}},

(3.21)

\big\lvert\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}}{p^{3}}\big\rvert\leq\frac{\lVert{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\not{g}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}}{p^{3}}\leq\frac{2\hskip 1.42262pta^{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty})}{\lvert u\rvert^{4}},

(3.22)

\big\lvert\frac{\underline{\eta}_{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}}{p^{3}}\big\rvert\leq\frac{\lVert{\underline{\eta}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\sqrt{\not{g}_{AB}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}}{p^{3}}\leq\frac{4a^{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262pt\sqrt{3\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty})}{\lvert u\rvert^{5}},

(3.23)

\big\lvert\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}\big\rvert\leq 2p^{3}(u_{\infty})\lVert{\underline{\omega}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq 2p^{3}(u_{\infty})\frac{a\hskip 1.42262ptO}{\lvert u\rvert^{3}}.

(3.24)

Putting everything together, we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{d|u|^{2}p^{4}}{du}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{2\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty})}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}+\frac{2\hskip 1.42262pta^{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty})}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}+\frac{4a^{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262pt\sqrt{3\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty})}{\lvert u\rvert^{3}}+\frac{8\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(u_{\infty})}{\lvert u\rvert^{3}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{17\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{4}}p^{3}(u_{\infty})O}{\lvert u\rvert^{3}}

(3.25)

and therefore

\displaystyle|u|^{2}p^{4}(u)\lesssim|u_{\infty}|^{2}p^{4}(u_{\infty})+\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lvert u\rvert}.

(3.26)

Moving on to
p^{A}
, in a way similar to what we did for
p^{4}
, we arrive at the following:

\begin{split}\frac{\text{d}\left(\lvert u\rvert^{2}p^{A}\right)}{\text{d}u}&=\hskip 1.42262pt\lvert u\rvert^{2}\bigg(\frac{-\not{\Gamma}^{A}_{BC}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{C}}{p^{3}}-\left(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}\right)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}-\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}}{p^{3}}-\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\hat{\chi}}^{A}_{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}-\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{2\hat{\chi}^{A}_{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}}{p^{3}}\\
&+\frac{(\not{\nabla}_{e_{B}}b)^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}}{p^{3}}-\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{b^{C}\hskip 1.42262pt\not{\Gamma}^{A}_{BC}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}}{p^{3}}-\hskip 1.42262pt2\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}\hskip 1.42262pt(\eta_{A}+\underline{\eta}_{A})\bigg)\end{split}

(3.27)

Using the bootstrap assumptions (
3.12
)-(
3.13
) along with the bound
\big\lvert\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}\big\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},
we arrive at the bound

\frac{\text{d}\left(\lvert u\rvert^{2}\lvert p^{A}\rvert\right)}{\text{d}u}\leq\big\lvert\frac{\text{d}\left(\lvert u\rvert^{2}p^{A}\right)}{\text{d}u}\big\rvert\leq\frac{20\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{A}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0)}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}.

(3.28)

Integrating (
3.28
), we arrive at

|u|^{2}|p^{A}(u)|\leq|u_{\infty}|^{2}|p^{A}(\infty)|+\frac{20\hskip 1.42262ptC_{p^{A}}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}(0)}{\lvert u\rvert}.

(3.29)

This concludes the bootstrap argument.

\square

3.3
Transport inequalities

Proposition 3.6

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
), there holds

\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lVert\phi\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}+\int_{\underline{u}^{\prime}}^{\underline{u}}\lVert\not{\nabla}_{4}\phi\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime\prime}})}\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime\prime},

(3.30)

\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lVert\phi\rVert_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}+\int_{\underline{u}^{\prime}}^{\underline{u}}\lVert\not{\nabla}_{4}\phi\rVert_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime\prime}})}\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime\prime},

(3.31)

for an
S_{u,\underline{u}}-
tangent tensor
\phi
of arbitrary rank.

Proof.

For a scalar
f
, we use the
1^{\text{st}}
variational formula to get

\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}\underline{u}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}f\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\mu_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}=\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\bigg(\frac{\text{d}f}{\text{d}\underline{u}}+\Omega^{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262ptf\bigg)\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\mu_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}=\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\Omega^{2}(e_{4}(f)+\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262ptf)\text{d}\mu_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}.

Taking
f=\lvert\phi\rvert_{\not{g}}^{2}
, applying the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality on the sphere and utilising the bounds on
\Omega
and
\operatorname{tr}\chi
, we have

2\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}\underline{u}}\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lVert{\not{\nabla}_{4}\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{O}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}^{2},

which implies

\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}\underline{u}}\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lVert{\not{\nabla}_{4}\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{O}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

An application of Grönwall’s inequality now yields the desired result. Taking
f=\lvert\phi\rvert_{\not{g}}^{4}
yields the corresponding result for the
L^{4}-
norm.
\square

Proposition 3.7

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
), there holds

\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lVert\phi\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u^{\prime}}^{u}\lVert\not{\nabla}_{3}\phi\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u^{\prime\prime}\underline{u})}}\text{d}u^{\prime\prime},

(3.32)

\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lVert\phi\rVert_{L^{4}(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u^{\prime}}^{u}\lVert\not{\nabla}_{3}\phi\rVert_{L^{4}(S_{u^{\prime\prime}\underline{u})}}\text{d}u^{\prime\prime},

(3.33)

for an
S_{u,\underline{u}}-
tangent tensor
\phi
of arbitrary rank.

Proof.

We again take the
1^{\text{st}}
variational formula in the
u-
direction to obtain

\underline{L}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\hskip 1.42262ptf\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\mu_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}=\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\big(\frac{\text{d}f}{\text{d}u}+\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hskip 1.42262ptf\big)\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\mu_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}=\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(e_{3}(f)+\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hskip 1.42262ptf)\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\mu_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}.

(3.34)

Working as before and using the fact that
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}<0
, we obtain

\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}u}\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lVert{\not{\nabla}_{3}\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

This implies

\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}u}\lVert{\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lVert{\not{\nabla}_{3}\phi}\rVert_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

which in turn implies the result.

\square

For the purposes of our analysis, it will be necessary to obtain and exploit certain finer bounds, along the
e_{3}
-direction, than those given by the above Proposition. These bounds will come from incorporating weights into the norms, weights which themselves depend on the coefficients in front of the linear term with a
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}
-factor. The essential observation is that, under the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
),
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}
may
be viewed as
-\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}
, so that
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}
is, roughly speaking,
O(\lvert u\rvert^{-2})
and hence, itself, integrable along the
u-
direction.

Proposition 3.8

(Evolution Lemma) We continue to work under the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
). Assume that
\phi
and
F
are
S_{u,\underline{u}}-
tangent tensor fields of rank
k
satisfying the following transport equation

\not{\nabla}_{3}\phi_{A_{1}\dots A_{k}}+\lambda_{0}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\phi_{A_{1}\dots A_{k}}=F_{A_{1}\dots A_{k}}.

Let
p\in\{2,4\}
. Denoting
\lambda_{1}:=2(\lambda_{0}-\frac{1}{p})
, the following inequality holds true:

\lvert u\rvert^{\lambda_{1}}\lVert\phi\rVert_{L^{p}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lvert u_{\infty}\rvert^{\lambda_{1}}\lVert\phi\rVert_{L^{p}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\lvert u^{\prime}\rvert^{\lambda_{1}}\lVert F\rVert_{L^{p}(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}u^{\prime}.

Proof.

This is the same as in
[
3
]
, the only exception being the different bootstrap assumption on
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}
.

\square

In addition, as we work in a low regularity regime, we also have to establish so-called
codimension-1 trace inequalities
, in order to control the
L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})-
norm of a tensorfield in terms of
L^{2}(H/\underline{H})-
norms.

Proposition 3.9
(Codimension-1 trace inequality along incoming null hypersurfaces)

Let the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
hold, and suppose the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
) are in force. Let
\varphi
be a scalar (or tensorial) function defined on the domain of dependence associated with the double null foliation, and suppose that
\varphi
is sufficiently regular so that the quantities below are well-defined. Then the following codimension-one trace inequality holds:

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{4}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}+\|\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}\left(\left\||u^{\prime}|^{-1}\varphi\right\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}+\|\not{\nabla}\varphi\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}\right).

(3.35)

Proof.

We assume the setting of Theorem
1.2
, including the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
), and work in the double null foliation framework. Let
\varphi
be a scalar (or more generally tensorial) field defined on the spacetime domain under consideration. All norms and volume forms are defined with respect to the induced geometric structures on the spheres
S_{u,\underline{u}}
and the incoming null hypersurface
\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}
.

We begin by applying the geometric isoperimetric inequality on the sphere
S_{u,\underline{u}}
:

\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}|\varphi|^{6}\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\lesssim\left(\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}|\varphi|^{4}\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\right)\left(\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(|u|^{-2}|\varphi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\varphi|^{2}\right)\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\right),

(3.36)

where
d\mu_{\not{g}}
denotes the volume form induced on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
by the metric
\not{g}
. Integrating (
3.36
) in
u^{\prime}\in[u_{\infty},u]
along the incoming null hypersurface
\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}
, we obtain:

\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}}}|\varphi|^{6}\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\,du^{\prime}\lesssim\sup_{u^{\prime}\in[u_{\infty},u]}\left(\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}}}|\varphi|^{4}\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\right)\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}}}\left(|u^{\prime}|^{-2}|\varphi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\varphi|^{2}\right)\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\,du^{\prime}.

(3.37)

Next, we estimate the supremum in the right-hand side of (
3.37
) via a transport inequality derived from integrating along the incoming null generator
\not{\nabla}_{3}
on
\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}
. Using the fundamental theorem of calculus and the structure of null hypersurfaces, we compute:

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|^{4}_{L^{4}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\frac{d}{du^{\prime}}\left(\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}}}|\varphi|^{4}\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\right)du^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\|\varphi\|^{4}_{L^{4}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}}}\left(4|\varphi|^{2}\langle\varphi,\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi\rangle+\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}|\varphi|^{4}\right)\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\,du^{\prime}.

(3.38)

To control the term involving
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}
, we decompose

\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}=\left(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|}\right)-\frac{2}{|u|},

and observe that the anomalous term
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|}
is integrable in
u^{\prime}
along
\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}
under the bootstrap assumptions. Applying Grönwall’s inequality (if needed) and using Cauchy–Schwartz and Hölder, we estimate:

\displaystyle\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}}}|\varphi|^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi|\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\,du^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{6}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}^{3}\|\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})},

and conclude from (
3.3
):

\|\varphi\|^{4}_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\|\varphi\|^{4}_{L^{4}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}+\|\varphi\|^{3}_{L^{6}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}\|\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}.

(3.39)

We now substitute the bound (
3.39
) into the right-hand side of (
3.37
) to deduce:

\displaystyle\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}}}|\varphi|^{6}\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\,du^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\|\varphi\|^{4}_{L^{4}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}+\|\varphi\|^{3}_{L^{6}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}\|\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}\right)\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}\left(|u^{\prime}|^{-2}|\varphi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\varphi|^{2}\right)\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\,du^{\prime}.

(3.40)

We now apply Young’s inequality to control the mixed term and deduce:

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\varphi|^{6}\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\,du^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}^{2}\left(\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}\left(|u|^{-2}|\varphi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\varphi|^{2}\right)\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\,du^{\prime}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\varphi\|^{4}_{L^{4}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}\left(|u|^{-2}|\varphi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\varphi|^{2}\right)\,d\mu_{\not{g}}\,du^{\prime}.

(3.41)

Substituting this estimate back into (
3.39
) and taking appropriate roots yields the desired inequality:

\|\varphi\|_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{4}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}+\|\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}\left(\||u|^{-1}\varphi\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}+\|\not{\nabla}\varphi\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}\right),

which completes the proof.

\square

Proposition 3.10

Let the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
hold, and suppose the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
) are in force. Let
\varphi
be a scalar (or tensorial) function defined on the domain of dependence associated with the double null foliation, and suppose that
\varphi
is sufficiently regular so that the quantities below are well-defined. The following scale-invariant codimension 1 trace inequality holds:

\displaystyle||\varphi||_{L^{4}_{sc}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim||\varphi||_{L^{4}_{sc}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}+||a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\underline{H})}\left(a^{-\frac{1}{4}}||\varphi||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\underline{H})}+||\not{\nabla}\varphi||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\underline{H})}\right).

(3.42)

Proof.

We begin by recalling the relevant scale-invariant norms and signature conventions. For a tensorial quantity
\varphi
defined on the 2-sphere
S_{u,\underline{u}}
in a double null foliation, let
s_{2}(\varphi)
denote the scale weight associated to
\varphi
under the rescaling symmetry. Then, by definition of the scale-invariant
L^{p}
norm, we have:

\|\varphi\|_{L^{p}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}=\frac{a^{s_{2}(\varphi)}}{|u|^{2s_{2}(\varphi)+\frac{p-2}{p}}}\|\varphi\|_{L^{p}_{sc}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(3.43)

where
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}_{sc}}
denotes the scale-invariant norm. The scale weights for derivatives obey:

s_{2}(\not{\nabla}\varphi)=s_{2}(\varphi)+\frac{1}{2},\quad s_{3}(\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi)=s_{2}(\varphi)+1.

(3.44)

Let us now recall from Proposition
3.9
the scale-invariant
L^{4}
inequality for
\varphi
derived previously, restated with the above normalization:

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{4}(S_{u_{\infty},\underline{u}})}+\|\not{\nabla}_{3}\varphi\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}\left(\||u|^{-1}\varphi\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}+\|\not{\nabla}\varphi\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}((\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}^{(u_{\infty},u)})}\right).

(3.45)
