\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{6}=i-1}\mathcal{D}^{i_{1}}\psi_{g}^{i_{2}}\mathcal{D}^{i_{3}}\lvert u\rvert\mathcal{D}^{i_{4}}(\eta,\underline{\eta})\mathcal{D}^{i_{5}}(\eta,\underline{\eta})\mathcal{D}^{i_{6}}\mu.

(8.70)

We proceed on a term by term basis. For the first term (keeping in mind that the very first operator from
\mathcal{D}_{i_{4}+1}
acting on
\operatorname{tr}\chi
is
a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\Big\lVert{\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i-1}\mathcal{D}^{i_{1}}(\psi_{g},\lvert u\rvert\omega)^{i_{2}}\mathcal{D}^{i_{3}}(\eta,\underline{\eta})\mathcal{D}^{i_{4}+1}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\Big\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime},

(8.71)

we distinguish cases. If
i_{4}=i-1
, then we control the term by a bootstrap on the elliptic norm of
\operatorname{tr}\chi
and use the improvements on
\lVert{\eta,\underline{\eta}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
that we obtained in Section
6
(note that in the non-borderline cases, we can just as well use the bootstrap assumptions instead of the improvements):

\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert{(\eta,\underline{\eta})\mathcal{D}^{i}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\lesssim\frac{O_{\text{ell}}[\operatorname{tr}\chi]C(\mathcal{R},\underline{\mathcal{R}},\mathcal{V},\underline{\mathcal{V}})}{\lvert u\rvert}\lesssim 1.

(8.72)

If
i_{4}=i-2=1
, then we bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\big(\lVert{\mathcal{D}(\eta,\underline{\eta})\mathcal{D}^{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}+\lVert{(\psi_{g},\lvert u\rvert\underline{\omega})(\eta,\underline{\eta})\mathcal{D}^{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\big)\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}O\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{4}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}+\frac{O^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{O}{\lvert u\rvert}\big(\lVert{\mathcal{D}\not{\nabla}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\not{\nabla}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\lVert{\mathcal{D}\not{\nabla}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})}\big)+1

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{O}{\lvert u\rvert}(O^{\frac{1}{2}}O_{\text{ell}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+O)+1\lesssim 1.

(8.73)

If
i_{4}=i-2=0,
then we bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\big(\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}(\eta,\underline{\eta})(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}+\lVert{(\psi_{g},\lvert u\rvert\underline{\omega})\mathcal{D}(\eta,\underline{\eta})(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\big)\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}(\psi_{g},\lvert u\rvert\underline{\omega})(\eta,\underline{\eta})(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})\operatorname{tr}\chi)}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\big)\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}\lesssim 1,

(8.74)

where we have bounded
\mathcal{D}^{2}(\eta,\underline{\eta})
in
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}
and the
(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})\operatorname{tr}\chi
in
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}
since, using Sobolev embedding, we have

\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lVert{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\lesssim\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\lVert{(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})^{1+i}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}.

The terms
\lVert{\not{\nabla}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})},\lVert{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla}^{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}
are controlled by estimates in the previous sections, whereas the term

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}(\eta,\underline{\eta})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\lVert{a\not{\nabla}^{3}\operatorname{tr}\chi}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}\sup_{0\leq\underline{u}^{\prime}\leq 1}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}(\eta,\underline{\eta})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert a\not{\nabla}^{3}\operatorname{tr}\chi\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime\prime}})}\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime\prime}

is controlled by the elliptic control we have on
\mathcal{D}^{2}\not{\nabla}\operatorname{tr}\chi
. For the second term in
\mathcal{D}^{i_{1}}\psi_{g}^{i_{2}}\mathcal{D}^{i_{3}}M_{0}
, similarly, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{i_{1}}\psi_{g}^{i_{2}}\mathcal{D}^{i_{3}}\hat{\chi}\mathcal{D}^{i_{4}}\not{\nabla}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert{\hat{\chi}\mathcal{D}^{2}\not{\nabla}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}+1

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}O}{\lvert u\rvert}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert{\not{\nabla}\mathcal{D}^{2}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}+1\lesssim 1.

(8.75)

This term will be absorbed by Grönwall’s inequality in the end, so can be ignored for all purposes. Note that here there is no need to use the improved estimate from Remark
5
for
\lVert{\hat{\chi}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
. as the bootstrap assumption only suffices. Similarly, the rest of the terms in

\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{i_{1}}\psi_{g}^{i_{2}}\mathcal{D}^{i_{3}}M_{0}}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}

are bounded above by
1.
Finally, for the rest of the terms in (
8.2
), we see that they are also bounded above by 1 using the bootstrap assumptions. Ultimately, we obtain

\sum_{i=0}^{2}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\mu}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}\lesssim 1.

(8.76)

We now recall the div-curl system :

\mbox{div}\mkern-19.0mu/\,\,\,\,\eta=-\mu-\rho+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\hat{\chi}}+\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}},

\mbox{curl}\mkern-19.0mu/\,\,\,\,\eta=\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\underline{\hat{\chi}}\wedge\hat{\chi}.

Making use of Proposition
8.1
, we thus obtain

\displaystyle\lVert{a\not{\nabla}^{3}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2}\big(\lVert{(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})^{i}\mu}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\lVert{(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})^{i}(\rho,\sigma)}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\lVert{(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})^{i}(\hat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\hat{\chi}})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lVert{(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})^{i}(\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\big)+\sum_{i\leq 1}\lVert{(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})^{i}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

(8.77)

Integrating along the
\underline{u}-
direction and raising to the second power, we arrive at

\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{a\not{\nabla}^{3}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(H_{u}^{(0,\underline{u})})}\lesssim 1+\mathcal{R}.

This proves the claim of the Proposition for the case
\mathcal{D}^{2}=(a^{\frac{1}{2}}\not{\nabla})^{2}
. For a general
\mathcal{D}^{2},
we obtain a new div-curl system of the form

\mbox{div}\mkern-19.0mu/\,\,\,\,\mathcal{D}^{2}\eta=\mathcal{D}^{2}(-\mu-\rho+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\hat{\chi}}+\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\chi\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}})+[\mbox{div}\mkern-19.0mu/\,\,\,\,,\mathcal{D}^{2}]\eta=\mathcal{D}^{2}(-\mu-\rho+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\hat{\chi}}+\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\chi\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}})+\hskip 1.42262pt\text{terms of order $\leq 2$},

\mbox{curl}\mkern-19.0mu/\,\,\,\,\mathcal{D}^{2}\eta=\mathcal{D}^{2}(\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\underline{\hat{\chi}}\wedge\hat{\chi})+\hskip 1.42262pt\text{terms of order $\leq 2$}.

As such, there holds

\lVert{\not{\nabla}\mathcal{D}^{2}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}(\mu,\rho,\sigma,\hat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\hat{\chi}},\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\hskip 1.42262pt\text{lower-order terms}.

(8.78)

Finally,

\displaystyle\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\not{\nabla}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert{\not{\nabla}\mathcal{D}^{2}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\lVert{[\not{\nabla},\mathcal{D}^{2}]\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}(\mu,\rho,\sigma,\hat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\hat{\chi}},\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262pt\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}})}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\hskip 1.42262pt\text{lower-order terms}.

(8.79)

Integrating along the
\underline{u}-
direction and raising to the second power, we arrive at

\frac{a}{\lvert u\rvert}\lVert{\mathcal{D}^{2}\not{\nabla}\eta}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(sc)}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim 1+\mathcal{R}.

\square

9
Estimates for the Vlasov matter

We begin the discussion on the Vlasov stress-energy tensor components
T_{\mu\nu}
, for up to three derivatives. These components, being functions of spacetime themselves, have well-defined norms with respect to the spacetime derivatives
\mathcal{D}
. However, from the point of view of the mass shell, they are non-local objects, in that they appear integrated over the momentum support. After having established the decay rates for the momentum components involved in this integral, we need to estimate the growth of certain derivatives of
f
on the mass shell. This essentially translates to a problem of controlling several error terms, arising from the non-commutativity of the vector fields in question with the geodesic spray
X
.

In this section, we control these Vlasov objects. First, we define the Vlasov norms that we want to control. Before we proceed to do so, we need to define the vertical and horizontal lifts associated with the tangent bundle. As
f
lives on
\mathcal{P}\subset T\mathcal{M}
, to estimate derivatives of
f
we use vector fields
\in\Gamma(TT\mathcal{M})
, much like the geodesic spray. There is a natural way to "lift" vector fields from
\Gamma(T\mathcal{M})
to
\Gamma(TT\mathcal{M})
(or
\Gamma(TT\mathcal{P}))
:

Definition 2

Given
v\in T_{x}\mathcal{M},
its
vertical lift
at
p\in T_{x}\mathcal{M}
,
\text{Ver}_{(x,p)}(v)
, is defined to be the vector tangent to the curve
c_{(x,p),V}:(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\to T\mathcal{M}
given by

c_{(x,p),v}(s)=(x,p+sv),

at
s=0
.

Similarly, we introduce the notion of a
horizontal lift
of a vector field on
\mathcal{M}
.

Definition 3

Let
c:(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\to\mathcal{M}
be a curve on
\mathcal{M}
with
c(0)=x,c^{\prime}(0)=v
. Extend
p
to a vector field along
c
by parallel transport using the Levi-Civita connection of
g
on
\mathcal{M}
. The
horizontal lift
of
v
at
(x,p)
denoted
\text{Hor}_{(x,p)}(v)
is then defined to be the tangent vector to the curve
c_{(x,p),H}:(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\to T\mathcal{M}
given by
c_{(x,p),H}(s)=(c(s),p)
at
s=0
,

\text{Hor}_{(x,p)}(v)=c_{(x,p),H}^{\prime}(0).

Given the coordinates

p^{1},\dots,p^{4}
on
T_{x}\mathcal{M}
conjugate to
e_{1},\dots,e_{4}
, the double null frame on
\mathcal{M}
, one has a frame for
T\mathcal{M}
given by
(e_{1},\dots,e_{4},p^{1},\dots p^{4})
. If
v\in T_{x}\mathcal{M}
is written as
v=v^{\mu}e_{\mu}
, then

\text{Ver}_{(x,p)}(v)=v^{\mu}\partial_{p^{\mu}},

with a similar expression for
\text{Hor}_{(x,p)}(v):

\text{Hor}_{(x,p)}(v)=v^{\mu}e_{\mu}-v^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{p^{\lambda}}.

We note that, after restricting
X
to
\mathcal{P}\subset T\mathcal{M}
, the Vlasov equation rewrites as

{\left.\kern-1.2ptX\right|_{\mathcal{P}}}f=p^{\mu}e^{\mu}(f)-\Gamma^{\hat{\lambda}}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{\hat{\lambda}}}f=0.

Here
\hat{\lambda}
runs over
1,2,3
and
\overline{p}^{1},\overline{p}^{2},\overline{p}^{3}
denote the restrictions of
p^{1},p^{2},p^{3}
to
\mathcal{P}
, while
\partial_{\overline{p}^{1}},\partial_{\overline{p}^{2}},\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}
denote the partial derivatives with respect to the restricted coordinate system
(\overline{p}^{1},\overline{p}^{2},\overline{p}^{3})
. It is easy to see that

\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}=\partial_{p^{A}}+\frac{\not{g}_{AB}p^{B}}{2p^{3}}\partial_{p^{4}},\hskip 5.69054pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}=\partial_{p^{3}}-\frac{p^{4}}{p^{3}}\partial_{p^{4}}.

Finally, notice that

X_{(x,p)}=Hor_{(x,p)}(p).

(9.1)

Recall the vector fields
V_{1},\dots V_{6},

\displaystyle V_{(A)}:=Hor(e_{A})-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}},\hskip 1.42262ptV_{(3)}:=Hor(e_{4}),\hskip 8.53581ptV_{(4)}:=p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}-\lvert u\rvert Hor(e_{3}),\hskip 8.53581ptV_{(4+A)}:=\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}},

(9.2)

together with the vector field

V_{(0)}:=\lvert u\rvert Hor(e_{3}).

(9.3)

We further introduce a frame
F
on the mass shell
\mathcal{P}
given by

\displaystyle F_{A}=e_{A},\hskip 1.42262pt\hskip 1.42262ptF_{3}=\lvert u\rvert\partial_{u},\hskip 1.42262pt\hskip 1.42262ptF_{4}=\partial_{v},F_{4+A}=\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}},F_{7}=p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}.

(9.4)

This will be the frame in which the commutators will be calculated. Introduce, for convenience, the set

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}:=\{V_{(A)},V_{(3)},V_{(4)},\lvert u\rvert V_{(4+A)},\lvert u\rvert V_{(0)}\},

(9.5)

where
A=1,2
. Also, define for
k_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the total Vlasov norms:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}_{0}:=\sup_{u,\underline{u}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\sup_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}f,

(9.6)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}_{1}:=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\sup_{u,\underline{u}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V_{(i)}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}+\sum_{i\in\{0,5,6\}}\sup_{u,\underline{u}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}||u|V_{(i)}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}},

(9.7)

\mathfrak{V}_{2}:=\sum_{\tilde{V}_{(1)},\tilde{V}_{(2)}\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}\sup_{u,\underline{u}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\lvert\tilde{V}_{(1)}\tilde{V}_{(2)}f\rvert^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\hskip 1.42262pt\sqrt{\det\not{g}}d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2},

(9.8)

\mathfrak{V}_{3}:=\sum_{\tilde{V}_{(1)},\tilde{V}_{(2)},\tilde{V}_{(3)}\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}\sup_{u}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\lvert\tilde{V}_{(1)}\tilde{V}_{(2)}\tilde{V}_{(3)}f\rvert^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\hskip 1.42262pt\sqrt{\det\not{g}}d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{d}\underline{u}^{\prime}.

(9.9)

Once we have controlled these norms, we can easily estimate derivatives of
T_{\mu\nu}
by the following relations
between
8
8

8

Herein by
e_{\mu},\mu=1,2,3,4
we mean
e_{\mu}
extended as a vector field
\in\Gamma(TT\mathcal{P})
.
the
e_{\mu}
and the vector fields
V
(here
A=1,2
):

\displaystyle e_{A}=

\displaystyle V_{(A)}+\lvert u\rvert^{2}\bigg(\frac{\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{AC}p^{C}}{p^{3}}+{\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{A}}^{B}+\frac{{\chi_{A}}^{B}p^{4}}{p^{3}}\bigg)V_{(4+B)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}{2}\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}V_{(4+A)}+\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\chi_{AB}p^{B}}{p^{3}}-\underline{\eta}_{A}\bigg)(V_{(4)}+V_{(0)}),

(9.10)

\lvert u\rvert e_{3}=\big(1+\lvert u\rvert\frac{\eta_{A}p^{A}}{p^{3}}+\lvert u\rvert\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\omega}\big)V_{(0)}+\lvert u\rvert^{2}\bigg(\frac{({\underline{\chi}_{B}}^{A}-e_{B}(b^{A}))p^{B}+2\eta^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}}{p^{3}}\bigg)\lvert u\rvert V_{(4+A)}+\lvert u\rvert\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262ptV_{4},

(9.11)

e_{4}=V_{(3)}+\lvert u\rvert^{2}\left(\frac{{\chi_{B}}^{A}p^{B}}{p^{3}}+2\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\eta}^{A}\right)V_{(4+A)},

(9.12)

\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{p}^{A}}=\frac{|u|^{2}}{p^{3}}V_{(4+A)},~\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{p}^{3}}=V_{(4)}+V_{(0)}.

(9.13)

In order to estimate these
\mathfrak{V}-
norms, we integrate the transport equations

\displaystyle X[V^{(i)}f]=E_{i},

(9.14)

for
i=1,2,3
, where by
V^{(i)}
we denote the presence of
n

V-
vector fields and
E^{(i)}
are the corresponding error terms.
For this, we need the following important integration lemma, which is essentially a version of the Reynolds transport theorem adapted to the mass shell.

Lemma 9.1
(Transport inequality on the mass shell)

Assume that the bootstrap assumptions (
2.80
) and (
8.45
) hold. Let
\mathcal{P}\subset TM
denote the future mass shell in the tangent bundle of a Lorentzian manifold
(\mathcal{M},g)
, equipped with the canonical Sasaki metric
\widetilde{g}
. Let
\varphi:\mathcal{P}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth scalar function, and consider the squared modulus
|\varphi|^{2}
on
\mathcal{P}
. We define the following scalar functionals on level sets of the foliation
\{S_{u,\underline{u}}\}
and on the null foliation of
\mathcal{M}
:

A(s):=\int_{\pi^{-1}(x(s))}|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}},

(9.15)

B(s):=\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\int_{\pi^{-1}(x(s))}|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,dS_{u,\underline{u}}(x),

(9.16)

C(s):=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\int_{\pi^{-1}(x(s))}|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,dS_{u,\underline{u}}(x)\,d\underline{u},

(9.17)

where
\pi:\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{M}
denotes the basepoint projection,
\not{g}
denotes the induced Riemannian metric on the 2-spheres
S_{u,\underline{u}}
, and
dS_{u,\underline{u}}
is the Riemannian measure associated to
\not{g}
. The variable
s\in\mathbb{R}
is an affine parameter along the integral curves of the geodesic spray vector field
X\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
, i.e.,
\gamma(s)=\exp_{s}(x_{0},p_{0})
solves
\frac{d\gamma}{ds}=X(\gamma(s))
. Then the following integral inequalities hold along the geodesic flow:

A(s)\lesssim A(s_{\infty})+\int_{s_{\infty}}^{s}\left(\int_{\pi^{-1}(x(s^{\prime}))}\varphi\cdot X[\varphi]\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)ds^{\prime},

(9.18)

B(s)\lesssim B(s_{\infty})+\int_{s_{\infty}}^{s}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\int_{\pi^{-1}(x)}\varphi\cdot X[\varphi]\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,dS_{u,\underline{u}}(x)\,ds^{\prime},

(9.19)

C(s)\lesssim C(s_{\infty})+\int_{s_{\infty}}^{s}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\int_{\pi^{-1}(x)}\varphi\cdot X[\varphi]\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,dS_{u,\underline{u}}(x)\,d\underline{u}\,ds^{\prime}.

(9.20)

Here, again,
X
is the geodesic spray, i.e. the Hamiltonian vector field associated with the kinetic Hamiltonian
H(x,p)=\frac{1}{2}g_{x}(p,p)
and the implied constants depend only on the dimension of the spacetime.

Proof.

Let
X
denote the generator of the geodesic flow on the mass shell
\mathcal{P}_{x}\subset T_{x}\mathcal{M}
, defined by
X=p^{\mu}\partial_{x^{\mu}}-\Gamma^{\hat{\lambda}}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{\hat{\lambda}}}
, and consider the evolution of the quadratic observable

A(s):=\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}},

(9.21)

along the integral curves of
X
, where
\varphi
is compactly supported in
\mathcal{P}_{x}
and smooth. By applying the transport theorem on the evolving hypersurface
\mathcal{P}_{x}
embedded in
T_{x}M
, we compute the total derivative of
A(s)
along the flow:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}A(s)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}X(|\varphi|^{2})\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}+\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|\varphi|^{2}\frac{1}{\mu}X[\mu]\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}},

(9.22)

where
\mu=\frac{\sqrt{\det\not{g}}}{p^{3}}
and

\frac{1}{\mu}X[\mu]=\left(\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\mu\alpha}p^{\mu}+\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{3}}\right).

Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}A(s)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\varphi X[\varphi]\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}+\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\left(\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\mu\alpha}p^{\mu}+\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{3}}\right)|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}.

(9.23)

We now analyze the two error terms arising from the divergence computation. First, we expand the contraction:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\mu\alpha}p^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\Gamma^{A}_{3A}p^{3}+\Gamma^{A}_{4A}p^{4}+\Gamma^{A}_{BA}p^{B}+\Gamma^{4}_{34}p^{3}+\Gamma^{4}_{44}p^{4}+\Gamma^{4}_{A4}p^{A}

\displaystyle+\Gamma^{3}_{33}p^{3}+\Gamma^{3}_{43}p^{4}+\Gamma^{3}_{A3}p^{A}.

(9.24)

By introducing the standard null structure coefficients on the mass shell, and expressing the angular connection coefficients
\Gamma^{A}_{BA}
in terms of
\not{g}
, we rewrite the above as

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\mu\alpha}p^{\mu}=\left(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}-e_{A}(b^{A})\right)p^{3}+\operatorname{tr}\chi\,p^{4}+\not{\Gamma}^{A}_{BA}p^{B}+2\underline{\omega}p^{3}+(\underline{\eta}_{A}-\eta_{A})p^{A},

(9.25)

which we abbreviate as

\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\mu\alpha}p^{\mu}=\left(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}-e_{A}(b^{A})\right)p^{3}+r,

with a remainder term
r
satisfying

|r|\lesssim\frac{a^{1/2}\Gamma}{|u|^{2}},

using the bootstrap assumptions and
L^{\infty}
estimates for the Ricci coefficients. The leading term
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\,p^{3}
decays only like
|u|^{-1}
, and thus is not integrable in the
u
-direction. However,
we exploit a favorable cancellation
by rewriting:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\mu\alpha}p^{\mu}|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\left(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|}-e_{A}(b^{A})\right)p^{3}|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{|u|}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}p^{3}|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}+\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}r|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}.

(9.26)

The cancellation of the leading borderline term now yields

\left|\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\mu\alpha}p^{\mu}|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right|\lesssim\frac{a^{1/2}\Gamma}{|u|^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|\varphi|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}.

Notice that the appearance of a decay term is essentially a consequence of the fact that the measure of the spheres
S_{u,\underline{u}}
is strictly decreasing along the incoming direction.
Next, we estimate the term
\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}/p^{3}
. Using the null frame decomposition of the connection coefficients, we compute

\displaystyle\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}

\displaystyle=\Gamma^{3}_{44}(p^{4})^{2}+2\Gamma^{3}_{43}p^{4}p^{3}+2\Gamma^{3}_{4A}p^{4}p^{A}+\Gamma^{3}_{33}(p^{3})^{2}+2\Gamma^{3}_{3A}p^{3}p^{A}+\Gamma^{3}_{AB}p^{A}p^{B}

\displaystyle=2\underline{\omega}(p^{3})^{2}+(\eta_{A}-\underline{\eta}_{A})p^{A}p^{3}+\chi_{AB}p^{A}p^{B}.

(9.27)

Therefore, applying the pointwise estimates for Ricci coefficients, we obtain

\left|\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\right|\lesssim\frac{a\Gamma}{|u|^{3}}+\frac{a^{1/2}\Gamma}{|u|^{2}}.

Substituting these bounds into (
9.23
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}A(s)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\varphi X[\varphi]\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}+\frac{a^{1/2}\Gamma}{|u|^{2}}A(s)+\frac{a\Gamma}{|u|^{3}}A(s).

(9.28)

Applying Grönwall’s inequality and invoking Proposition
2.1
, together with the bootstrap assumptions and the smoothness of
\varphi
, we deduce that

A(s)\lesssim A(s_{\infty})+\int_{s_{\infty}}^{s}\left(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\varphi X[\varphi]\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)ds^{\prime}.

The estimates for the associated functionals
B(s)
and
C(s)
proceed analogously and are omitted for brevity.

\square

Now we want to apply these transport inequalities systematically to control up to three derivatives of the Vlasov distribution function. Before moving to the estimates, we recall one more remark.

Remark 8

One can bound the spacetime (or over
S_{u,\underline{u}}
)
L^{2}
-norm of the Vlasov stress-energy tensor components by the
L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}
norm of the same tensor components, using Hölder’s inequality and the decay properties of the momentum support obtained in subsection
3.2
. Indeed, there holds

\displaystyle\big\lVert\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\varphi\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\big\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\varphi\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)\left(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\varphi\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\text{d}\theta^{1}\text{d}\theta^{2}.

Now we can use Cauchy-Schwarz for the integral to get:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\varphi\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\varphi^{2}\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|u|}\left(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\varphi^{2}\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

Therefore, we have under the decay of the momentum support (
3.5
),

\displaystyle||\int_{P}\varphi\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{p}\varphi^{2}\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}.

(9.29)

Now we state one of the most vital aspects of the Vlasov estimates, namely that of the expressions for the commutators
[X,V]
for each of the
V-
vector fields.

Remark 9

It is of importance to include
[X,V_{(0)}]
, for the sake of higher derivative estimates. However,

[X,V_{(0)}]=-[X,V_{(4)}]+[X,p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}].

(9.38)

Therefore, we omit its explicit expression.

Proof.

The proof is a consequence of direct calculations, keeping careful track of all the cancellations. Recall

X=p^{\mu}e_{\mu}-\Gamma^{A}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}-\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}.

We perform the commutation calculations in the holonomic
C
-basis
9
9

9

This was deemed most practical, because in this holonomic basis the commutators enjoy the simple identity

[X,V]^{i}=X(V^{i})-V(X^{i}),

where
F^{i}
denotes the
i-
th component of a vector field
F
.

\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta^{A}},\frac{\partial}{\partial u},\frac{\partial}{\partial\underline{u}},\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{p}^{A}},\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{p}^{3}}\}_{A=1}^{2}

In this
C
-basis, the components of the vector field
X
read
10
10

10

Recall the choice of frame (
2.1
).

X=\big(p^{3}b^{A}+p^{A},p^{3},\frac{p^{4}}{\Omega^{2}},-\Gamma^{A}_{\nu\lambda}p^{\nu}p^{\lambda},-\Gamma^{3}_{\nu\lambda}p^{\nu}p^{\lambda}\big).

(9.39)

First, consider the vector field
V_{(A)}
. We have
V_{(A)}=\text{Hor}(e_{A})-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}},
hence

V_{(A)}=\big({\delta_{A}}^{B},0,0,-(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}{\delta_{A}}^{B}),-\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}\big).

For the first two components, there holds

\displaystyle[X,V_{(A)}]^{B}=

\displaystyle X({\delta_{A}}^{B})-V_{(A)}(p^{3}b^{B}+p^{B})=-p^{3}e_{A}(b^{B})+\big(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}{\delta_{A}}^{B}\big)-\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}b^{B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{AC}p^{C}+{\chi_{A}}^{B}p^{4}+\big({\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{A}}^{B}-e_{A}(b^{B})+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}{\delta_{A}}^{B}\big)p^{3}-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{AC}p^{C}b^{B}+\underline{\eta}_{A}p^{3}b^{B}.

(9.40)

For the third component, there holds

\displaystyle[X,V_{(A)}]^{3}=-\text{Hor}(e_{A})(p^{3})=\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}=\chi_{AB}p^{B}-\underline{\eta}_{A}p^{3}.

(9.41)

For the fourth component,

\displaystyle[X,V_{(A)}]^{4}=

\displaystyle-\text{Hor}(e_{A})\big(\frac{p^{4}}{\Omega^{2}}\big)=\frac{2}{\Omega^{2}}e_{A}(\log\Omega)p^{4}+\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big(\Gamma^{C}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}\frac{\not{g}_{CD}p^{D}}{2p^{3}}-\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}\frac{p^{4}}{p^{3}}+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\frac{\not{g}_{AB}p^{B}}{2p^{3}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\Omega^{2}}(\eta_{A}+\underline{\eta}_{A})p^{4}+\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\bigg(\big[\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AB}p^{B}+\big({\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{A}}^{C}+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}{\delta_{A}}^{C}\big)p^{3}+{\chi_{A}}^{C}p^{4}\big]\frac{\not{g}_{CD}p^{D}}{2p^{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\chi_{AB}p^{B}-\underline{\eta}_{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}\big)\frac{p^{4}}{p^{3}}.

(9.42)

For the fifth and sixth term, we have the following:

\displaystyle[X,Hor(e_{A})-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}]^{4+B}

\displaystyle=-X(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu}p^{\nu})+Hor(e_{A})(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-X\left(\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}{\delta_{A}}^{B}\right)-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(e_{A}(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu}))\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\mu}p^{\nu}}_{\text{I}}+\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\mu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})\Gamma^{B}_{A\lambda}+\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})\Gamma^{B}_{A\lambda}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\Gamma^{C}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})}_{\text{II}}-\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})+\left(\underbrace{-\frac{(p^{3})^{2}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}{\delta_{A}}^{B}}_{\text{I}}+\frac{{\delta_{A}}^{B}}{\lvert u\rvert}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}}_{\text{II}}.

(9.43)

Each pair of terms in brackets labelled (I) and (II) leads to a cancellation that improves the order of decay. Indeed, the first term in bracket (II), is
o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3})-{\underline{\chi}_{A}}^{C}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}({p^{\mu}p^{\nu}})+o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3})
. By itself, this term is only
o(\lvert u\rvert^{-2})
. When combined, however, with the second term in bracket
(II)
, the whole bracket becomes

(II)=-\frac{1}{2}p^{3}\hskip 1.42262pt\big(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}\big)\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})+o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3})=o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3}).

As for the terms in bracket (I), it is here where not only the issue of integrability, but also the issue of regularity in the terms is overcome. Indeed, notice that

e_{A}(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu})={R^{B}}_{\nu A\mu}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{A\epsilon}+\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{A\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\epsilon}+(\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{A\mu}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu A})\Gamma^{B}_{\epsilon\nu}.

(9.44)

In other words, throughout the entire argument,
derivatives of Christoffel symbols will always appear in differences that give rise to Riemann curvature components (up to terms of the form
\Gamma\Gamma
, which are harmless, both in terms of integrability and regularity)
.

As a consequence, the first term in
(I)
is
o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3})+\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{A\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\epsilon}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}
, where the latter fails to be
o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3})
precisely when
\mu=\nu=3
. In that case, the worst term is

\Gamma^{C}_{A3}\Gamma^{B}_{3C}p^{3}p^{3}\hskip 2.84526pt\text{(summation over C=1,2)}=\frac{1}{4}(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi})^{2}(p^{3})^{2}=o(\lvert u\rvert^{-2}).

When combined, however, with the second term in bracket (I), the whole bracket becomes

(I)=\frac{1}{4}(p^{3})^{2}\big(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}\big)\big(\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}-\frac{2}{\lvert u\rvert}\big){\delta_{A}}^{B}+o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3})=o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3}).

All the other terms in the commutator are also
o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3})
. As a consequence

[X,V_{(A)}]^{4+B}=o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3})\hskip 8.53581pt\text{(in the C basis)},

(9.45)

which is
o(\lvert u\rvert^{-2})
in the
F-
basis, where
F_{4+A}=\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}
.

For the seventh term, we also have

\displaystyle[X,Hor(e_{A})-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}]^{7}

\displaystyle=-X(\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu}p^{\nu})+Hor(e_{A})(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(e_{A}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu}))\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\mu}p^{\nu}}_{\text{I}}+\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\mu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}+\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\Gamma^{C}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})}_{\text{II}}-\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})\underbrace{-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}}_{\text{II}}=o(\lvert u\rvert^{-2}).

(9.46)

This follows similarly after writing

e_{A}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu})={R^{3}}_{\nu A\mu}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{A\epsilon}+\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{A\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\epsilon}+(\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{A\mu}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu A})\Gamma^{3}_{\epsilon\nu}.

and estimating term by term. We conclude that

\lvert XV_{A}f(x,p)\rvert=\lvert\hskip 1.42262pt[X,V_{(A)}](f)(x,p)\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{7}\lvert F_{j}(f)(x,p)\rvert\leq\frac{V}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}.

(9.47)

For
V_{(3)}=Hor(e_{4})
, we have

\text{Hor}(e_{4})=\big(0,0,\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}},-\Gamma^{A}_{4\nu}p^{\nu},-\Gamma^{3}_{4\nu}p^{\nu}\big).

(9.48)

Hence, we estimate

\displaystyle[X,V_{(3)}]^{B}=

\displaystyle-\text{Hor}(e_{4})(p^{3}b^{B}+p^{B})=-p^{3}e_{4}(b^{B})+\Gamma^{B}_{4\nu}p^{\nu}+\Gamma^{3}_{4\nu}p^{\nu}b^{B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-p^{3}\big(2(\underline{\eta}^{B}-\eta^{B})+{\hat{\chi}_{C}}^{B}b^{C}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262ptb^{B}-{\chi_{C}}^{B}b^{C}\big)+{\chi_{A}}^{B}p^{A}+2\underline{\eta}^{B}p^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\eta^{B}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}+{\hat{\chi}_{A}}^{B}p^{A}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\chi\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B},

(9.49)

\displaystyle[X,V_{(3)}]^{3}=-\text{Hor}(e_{4})(p^{3})=\Gamma^{3}_{4\nu}p^{\nu}=0.

(9.50)

For the fourth component, we have

\displaystyle[X,V_{(3)}]^{4}=

\displaystyle X\big(\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big)-\text{Hor}(e_{4})\big(\frac{p^{4}}{\Omega^{2}}\big)=p^{A}e_{A}\big(\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big)+p^{3}\hskip 1.42262pte_{3}\big(\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big)+\bcancel{p^{4}e_{4}\big(\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big)}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\bcancel{p^4 e_4\big(\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big)}+\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big(\Gamma^{C}_{4\nu}p^{\nu}\frac{\not{g}_{CD}p^{D}}{2p^{3}}-\Gamma^{3}_{4\nu}p^{\nu}\frac{p^{4}}{p^{3}}\big)=-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}(\eta_{A}+\underline{\eta}_{A})p^{A}

(9.51)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big[\underline{\omega}-\underline{\omega}\big(1-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big)\big]\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}+\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big({\chi_{B}}^{C}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{B}\frac{\not{g}_{CD}p^{D}}{2p^{3}}+2\underline{\eta}_{D}p^{D}\big).

(9.52)

Moving on, we have

\displaystyle[X,V_{(3)}]^{4+B}=

\displaystyle X(-\Gamma^{B}_{4\nu}p^{\nu})+Hor(e_{4})(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(e_{4}(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{B}_{4\nu}))p^{\mu}p^{\nu}+\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{4\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})+\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{4\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\Gamma^{C}_{4\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\Gamma^{3}_{4\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left({R^{B}}_{\nu 4\mu}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{4\epsilon}+\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{4\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\epsilon}+(\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{4\mu}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu 4})\Gamma^{B}_{\epsilon\nu}\right)p^{\mu}p^{\nu}+\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{4\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{4\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})-\Gamma^{C}_{4\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\Gamma^{3}_{4\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})=o(\lvert u\rvert^{-3}).

(9.53)

Finally, we have

\displaystyle[X,V_{(3)}]^{7}=

\displaystyle X(-\Gamma^{3}_{4\lambda}p^{\lambda})+Hor(e_{4})(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(e_{4}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{4\nu}))p^{\mu}p^{\nu}+\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{4\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})+\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{4\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\Gamma^{C}_{4\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\Gamma^{3}_{4\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left({R^{3}}_{\nu 4\mu}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{4\epsilon}+\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{4\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\epsilon}+(\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{4\mu}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu 4})\Gamma^{3}_{\epsilon\nu}\right)p^{\mu}p^{\nu}+\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{4\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{4\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})-\Gamma^{C}_{4\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\Gamma^{3}_{4\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\lambda}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu}).

(9.54)

Moving on to
V_{(4)}
, we have

V_{(4)}=\big(-\lvert u\rvert b^{A},-\lvert u\rvert,0,\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{A}_{3\nu}p^{\nu},\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}+p^{3}\big).

(9.55)

Therefore,

\displaystyle[X,V_{(4)}]^{B}=

\displaystyle X(-\lvert u\rvert b^{B})-p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{3}b^{B}+p^{B})+\lvert u\rvert\text{Hor}(e_{3})(p^{3}b^{B}+p^{B})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-p^{3}e_{3}(\lvert u\rvert b^{B})-\lvert u\rvert p^{4}e_{4}(b^{B})-p^{3}b^{B}+\lvert u\rvert p^{3}e_{3}(b^{B})-\lvert u\rvert(\Gamma^{B}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}+\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}b^{B})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\lvert u\rvert p^{4}(\underline{\eta}^{B}-\eta^{B})-\lvert u\rvert(\Gamma^{B}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}+\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}b^{B}).

(9.56)

Note two important things here. The first is the cancellation of the terms
-p^{3}e_{3}(\lvert u\rvert b^{B})-p^{3}b^{B}+\lvert u\rvert p^{3}e_{3}(b^{B})
. It is never the case that either one of
e_{3}(b^{B})
or
e_{4}(\Omega)
, objects for which we have no structure equations and whose appearance would create a loss of derivatives, can be part of a commutation term. Finally, note that
\Gamma^{B}_{3A}
appears whole and
e_{A}(b^{B})
does not appear by itself, as was clearly promised in the introduction. In addition, the lower order term
\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}b^{B}
containing the shift vector field cancels out while expressed in the
V
basis. We stress this point that working with the appropriate basis is extremely crucial. Moving on, we have

\displaystyle[X,V_{(4)}]^{3}=

\displaystyle X(-\lvert u\rvert)-(p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}-\lvert u\rvert\text{Hor}(e_{3}))(p^{3})=p^{3}-p^{3}+\lvert u\rvert\text{Hor}(e_{3})(p^{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}=\lvert u\rvert\eta_{A}p^{A}+\lvert u\rvert\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3},

(9.57)

\displaystyle[X,V_{(4)}]^{4}=

\displaystyle-(p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}-\lvert u\rvert\text{Hor}(e_{3}))\big(\frac{p^{4}}{\Omega^{2}}\big)=\frac{p^{4}}{\Omega^{2}}+\frac{\lvert u\rvert}{\Omega^{2}}\bigg[-\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}-(\Gamma^{A}_{3B}p^{B}+2\eta^{A}p^{4})\frac{\not{g}_{AD}p^{D}}{2p^{3}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\lvert u\rvert}{\Omega^{2}}(\eta_{A}p^{A}+\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3})\cdot\frac{p^{4}}{p^{3}}.

(9.58)

For the next two components, we have

\displaystyle[X,V_{(4)}]^{4+B}=

\displaystyle X(\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{B}_{3\nu}p^{\nu})-\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})+\lvert u\rvert e_{3}(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu})p^{\mu}p^{\nu}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{C}_{3\lambda}p^{\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{3\lambda}p^{\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lvert u\rvert\big(\left(e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{B}_{3\nu})-e_{3}(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu})\right)p^{\mu}p^{\nu}-\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}\left(p^{\lambda}\right)\Gamma^{B}_{3\lambda}-\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}\left(p^{\lambda}\right)\Gamma^{3}_{3\lambda}\big)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{p^{3}}{\Omega^{2}}\Gamma^{B}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}+\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})+\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\left(\Gamma^{C}_{3\lambda}p^{\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})+\Gamma^{3}_{3\lambda}p^{\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lvert u\rvert({R^{B}}_{\nu\mu 3}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{3\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\epsilon}+\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{3\epsilon}+(\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\mu 3}-\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{3\mu})\Gamma^{B}_{\epsilon\nu})-\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})\Gamma^{B}_{3\lambda}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})\Gamma^{3}_{3\lambda}-\frac{p^{3}}{\Omega^{2}}\Gamma^{B}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}+\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})+\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{C}_{3\lambda}p^{\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{3}_{3\lambda}p^{\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu}),

(9.59)

\displaystyle[X,V_{(4)}]^{7}=

\displaystyle X(p^{3}+\lvert u\rvert\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu}p^{\nu})+p^{3}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\lvert u\rvert Hor(e_{3})(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}-\frac{p^{3}}{\Omega^{2}}\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu}p^{\nu}+\lvert u\rvert\big(e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu})p^{\mu}p^{\nu}-\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{3\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})-\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{3\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})\big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle p^{3}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\lvert u\rvert\big(e_{3}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})p^{\mu}p^{\nu}-\Gamma^{C}_{3\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\Gamma^{3}_{3\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})\big).

(9.60)

For the last two vector fields
V_{(4+A)}=\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}
, we have

V_{(4+A)}=(0,0,0,\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},0),

(9.61)

hence direct computation yields

[X,V_{(4+A)}]^{B}=-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{3}b^{B}+p^{B})=-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}{\delta_{A}}^{B},

(9.62)

[X,V_{(4+A)}]^{3}=-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{3})=0,

(9.63)

[X,V_{(4+A)}]^{4}=-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}\big(\frac{p^{4}}{\Omega^{2}}\big)=-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\frac{\not{g}_{AD}p^{D}}{2\hskip 1.42262pt\Omega^{2}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}},

(9.64)

[X,V_{(4+A)}]^{4+B}=X\bigg(\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}{\delta_{A}}^{B}\bigg)+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})=\frac{{\delta_{A}}^{B}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\big(-\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}+\frac{2\hskip 1.42262pt(p^{3})^{2}}{\lvert u\rvert}\big)+\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu}).

(9.65)

[X,V_{(4+A)}]^{7}=-\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu})=o(\lvert u\rvert^{-2}).

(9.66)

Notice the disappearance of the
\frac{\partial}{\partial u}
component and hence no extra shift
b
term. Now, use the following relation between the
C
basis (lifted up to
\mathcal{P}
) and the
V
basis, for
A=1,2
:

\displaystyle e_{A}=

\displaystyle V_{(A)}+\lvert u\rvert^{2}\bigg(\frac{\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{AC}p^{C}}{p^{3}}+{\hat{\chi}_{A}}^{B}+\frac{{\chi_{A}}^{B}p^{4}}{p^{3}}\bigg)V_{(4+B)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}{2}\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}V_{(4+A)}+\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\chi_{AB}p^{B}}{p^{3}}-\underline{\eta}_{A}\bigg)(V_{(4)}+V_{(0)}),

(9.67)

\lvert u\rvert e_{3}=\big(1+\lvert u\rvert\frac{\eta_{A}p^{A}}{p^{3}}+\lvert u\rvert\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\omega}\big)V_{(0)}+\lvert u\rvert^{2}\bigg(\frac{({\underline{\chi}_{B}}^{A}-e_{B}(b^{A}))p^{B}+2\eta^{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{4}}{p^{3}}\bigg)\lvert u\rvert V_{(4+A)}+\lvert u\rvert\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262ptV_{4},

(9.68)

e_{4}=V_{(3)}+\lvert u\rvert^{2}\left(\frac{{\chi_{B}}^{A}p^{B}}{p^{3}}+2\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\eta}^{A}\right)V_{(4+A)},

(9.69)

\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{p}^{A}}=\frac{|u|^{2}}{p^{3}}V_{(4+A)},~\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{p}^{3}}=V_{(4)}+V_{(0)}.

(9.70)

This completes the proof of the commutation calculus.

\square

Remark 10
(Appearance of curvature)

First, note the most important observation made in this study. As pointed out by Taylor
[
27
]
, there appears to be a significant challenge in closing the regularity while deriving higher order estimates for the Vlasov distribution function
f
. Recall that the main ingredient that one possesses to estimate
f
is the transport equation

\displaystyle X[f]=0.

(9.71)

Consider estimating the third derivative of
f
; this is the desired regularity in the present framework. Taylor
[
27
]
observed that commutation of this equation with a vector field
V
introduces
V[\Gamma]
, introducing
V[e(b)]
, which requires control of four derivatives of the shift vector field
b
. Consequently, this would require control of four derivatives of the connection coefficients since
b
verifies an equation of the type

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}b\sim(\eta,\underline{\eta})+\chi\cdot b.

(9.72)

But this is impossible to close since the best we can do for the connection coefficients is to control them on null hypersurfaces up to three derivatives by means of elliptic estimates. This appears to be an obstruction to directly commuting the transport equation, even though heuristically speaking, four derivatives of
b
are at the same level as two derivatives of curvature-note components of
b
are essentially metric components. A similar phenomenon occurs for the connection coefficients
\not{\Gamma}
tangent to the
2-
spheres. However, Taylor’s observation ignores the full structure of the error terms-which he points out. We make the vital observation that the derivatives of the connection coefficients always appear in a combination that produces the curvature. For example, in the expression of
[X,V_{(4)}]
, one obtains
e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu})-e_{3}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})
. Note that this combination precisely yields the curvature through the elementary identity

\displaystyle e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{3\nu})-e_{3}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})=R^{3}~_{\nu\mu 3}-\Gamma^{\lambda}_{3\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\lambda}+\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{3\lambda}+(\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu 3}-\Gamma^{\lambda}_{3\mu})\Gamma^{3}_{\lambda\nu}.

(9.73)

Therefore, the undesirable terms of the type
V(e(b))
or
V(\not{\Gamma})
do not appear, and terms of the type
\Gamma\hskip 1.42262pt\Gamma
enjoy a higher decay property, or more precisely, integrable decay. This is pivotal throughout the present work.

Remark 11

We note another vital point about the structure of the commutators in proposition
9.1
. Notice that the shift vector field
b
always appears in the combination
e_{A}(b^{B})p^{A}+\Gamma^{B}_{3A}p^{A}
or as
-e_{A}(b^{B})p^{A}+\Gamma^{B}_{A3}p^{A}
(or instead of
p^{A}
, any other momentum variable
p^{\alpha}
appears in multiplication). Note that the term of the first type
e_{A}(b^{B})p^{A}+\Gamma^{B}_{3A}p^{A}
simplifies to
\underline{\chi}^{B}_{A}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{A}
while the second term
-e_{A}(b^{B})p^{A}+\Gamma^{B}_{A3}p^{A}
simply yields
\Gamma^{B}_{3A}p^{A}
. Luckily, we have separate
\not{\nabla}_{4}
equations for the trace and tracefree part of
\Gamma^{B}_{A3}
. In addition,
\not{\nabla}_{4}b
term can be expressed in terms of the Ricci coefficients by means of the transport equation for
b
.

9.1
First derivative control of Vlasov

Now we prove the first-order energy estimates for the Vlasov field. Motivated by the expression for the total first-order Vlasov norm
\mathfrak{V}_{1}
, we define certain
u-
dependent Vlasov norms. The first one is as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}_{1}(u):=\frac{|u|^{2}}{a^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{A=1,2}\sup_{\underline{u}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V_{A}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}+\sup_{\underline{u}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V_{4}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\underline{u}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V_{3}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}+\sum_{A=1,2}\sup_{\underline{u}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|\lvert u\rvert V_{4+A}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\underline{u}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}||u|V_{0}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\Bigg).

In addition to these uniform norms, we also need improved
L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})
-estimates of the
Vf
. We thus introduce:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}^{s}_{1}(u)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{A=1,2}\sup_{\underline{u}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\Bigg(\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V_{A}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\Bigg)dS_{u,\underline{u}}+\sup_{\underline{u}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V_{4}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}dS_{u,\underline{u}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{u}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V_{3}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}dS_{u,\underline{u}}+\sum_{A=1,2}\sup_{\underline{u}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|uV_{4+A}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}dS_{u,\underline{u}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{\underline{u}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}||u|V_{0}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}dS_{u,\underline{u}}\Bigg).

(9.74)

Also let us define
\mathfrak{V}_{1}(u_{\infty})=\mathcal{V}^{0}
and
\mathfrak{V}^{s}_{1}(u_{\infty})=\mathcal{V}^{0}_{s}

Remark 12

Note that the assumption on the initial data in Theorem
1.2
implies control of both
\mathcal{V}_{1}
and
\mathcal{V}^{S}_{1}
at
u=u_{\infty}
.

Proposition 9.2
(First derivative estimates for the distribution function)

Let
f\colon\mathcal{P}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
denote the distribution function associated to a solution of the Einstein–Vlasov system on a globally hyperbolic spacetime
(\mathcal{M},g)
, and let
\mathfrak{V}_{1}
denote the
L^{2}(\mathcal{P}_{x})
-norm of the first-order geometric derivatives of
f
along the frame-adapted vector fields
V
as defined in (
9.1
). Let
\mathcal{V}^{0}
(
\mathcal{V}^{0}_{s}
, respectively) denote the initial norm of
\mathfrak{V}_{1}
(
\mathfrak{V}^{s}_{1}
, respectively) on the null hypersurface
H_{u_{\infty}}
. Then, under the bootstrap assumptions described in Section
3.1
, the following pointwise bound holds:

\mathfrak{V}_{1}\lesssim 1+\mathcal{V}^{0},\mathfrak{V}^{s}_{1}\lesssim\mathcal{V}^{0}_{s}.

Proof.

We prove these estimates by using the transport inequality
9.1
on the mass-shell. But before doing so, let us make the following bootstrap assumption on the Vlasov field. Assume that
\mathfrak{V}_{1}(u)
and
\mathfrak{V}^{s}_{1}(u)
verifies the following bootstrap assumptions

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}_{1}\leq\mathcal{V},~\mathfrak{V}^{s}_{1}\leq\mathcal{V}_{1}

(9.75)

where
\mathcal{V}
(
\mathcal{V}_{1}
, respectively) is large enough so that
\mathcal{V}^{0}\ll\mathcal{V}
(
\mathcal{V}^{0}_{s}\ll\mathcal{V}_{1}
, respectively) but also
\mathcal{V}\ll a^{\frac{1}{10}}
(
\mathcal{V}_{1}\ll a^{\frac{1}{10}}
, respectively). We only consider the cases for the vector fields that contain terms that are borderline dangerous. The estimates for the remaining vector fields follow in the exact same manner. More precisely, we consider one estimate in the unweighted category (e.g., for the vector field
V_{(A)}
) and one estimate for the weighted category (e.g., for the vector field
V_{(4+A)}
). Now we use the transport inequality from Lemma
9.1
to the entity
\frac{a^{2}}{|u|^{2}}\mathfrak{V}_{1}
. First, consider
\mathfrak{V}^{1,2}_{1}:=\sum_{A=1,2}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|V_{A}f|^{2}\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}
. Now recall the transport lemma
9.1
and set
A(s):=\mathfrak{V}^{1,2}_{1}(s)
to yield

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{1,2}_{1}(s)\lesssim\mathcal{V}^{1,2}_{1}(s_{\infty})+\int_{s_{\infty}}^{s}\Bigg(\int_{p}V_{A}fX[V_{A}f]\sqrt{\det(\not{g})}\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\Bigg)ds^{{}^{\prime}}

(9.76)

and estimate the error term involving
X[V_{A}f]
.

\displaystyle[X,V_{(A)}]f=\sum_{J=0}^{6}C^{J}_{A}V_{J}f

(9.77)

The coefficients
C^{J}_{A}
of the error terms can be obtained from the proposition
9.1
. First, recall the expression for
C^{0}_{B},~B=1,2

\displaystyle C^{0}_{B}

\displaystyle=\big[-p^{3}\hskip 1.42262pte_{A}(b^{B})+\big(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}{\delta_{A}}^{B}\big)\big]\big(\frac{\frac{1}{2}\chi_{BC}p^{C}}{p^{3}}-\underline{\eta}_{B}\big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lvert u\rvert}\big(2\hskip 1.42262pte_{A}(\log\Omega)\hskip 1.42262ptp^{3}+\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}\big)\big(1+\lvert u\rvert\frac{\eta_{C}p^{C}}{p^{3}}+|u|\underline{\omega}\big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bigg((e_{A}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu}))p^{\mu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\nu}+\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})+\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\Gamma^{C}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})\bigg)\frac{1}{p^{3}}.

(9.78)

Fix a double null foliation
(u,\underline{u},\theta^{A})
and the corresponding sphere
S_{u,\underline{u}}
with induced metric
\not{g}_{AB}
. We use frame vectorfields
e_{3},e_{4},e_{A}
as in the main text. Greek indices
\mu,\nu,\dots
run over
\{1,2,3,4\}
; capital Latin indices
A,B,C
run over
\{1,2\}
and are raised and lowered with
\not{g}
. The momentum fibre coordinates are
(p^{1},p^{2},p^{3})
with
p^{4}=p^{4}(p^{1},p^{2},p^{3})
determined by the mass-shell constraint; we abbreviate
\not{p}=(p^{1},p^{2})
. The momentum integration on each fibre is

\int_{P}\phi(p)\,dp:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\phi(p)\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{p^{3}}\,,

as in the body of the paper. We assume the bootstrap/pointwise bounds on the Ricci coefficients
\psi
and curvature components
\Psi
recorded elsewhere.

Under the hypotheses above, there exists a finite collection of smooth scalar functions
\{f^{i}_{j}\}
of the indicated geometric variables such that, schematically,

\begin{split}C^{0}_{B}&=\sum_{i}f^{i}_{1}(\psi)\,f^{i}_{2}(\Psi)\,f^{i}_{3}(p^{4})\,f^{i}_{4}(\not{p})\,f^{i}_{5}(\not{g})\,f^{i}_{6}(\not{g}^{-1})\\
&\quad+\;\not{g}\not{p}\Bigg(p^{4}\int_{P}f\,p^{3}\,dp\;+\;p^{3}\int_{P}f\,p^{4}\,dp\;+\;\not{p}\int_{P}f\,\not{g}\not{p}\,dp\Bigg)\\
&\quad+\;p^{4}p^{3}\int_{P}f\,\not{g}\not{p}\,dp\;+\;p^{3}p^{3}\int_{P}f\,p^{4}\not{g}\not{p}\,dp\;+\;\not{p}\,p^{3}\int_{P}f\,\not{g}\not{p}\,\not{g}\not{p}\,dp,\end{split}

(9.79)

where each
f^{i}_{1}(\psi)
is a polynomial in the Ricci coefficients

\psi\in\{\hat{\chi},\operatorname{tr}\chi,\underline{\hat{\chi}},\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\omega},\eta,\underline{\eta}\},

each
f^{i}_{2}(\Psi)
is a polynomial in the Weyl components

\Psi\in\{\alpha,\beta,\rho,\sigma,\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha}\},

and the remaining
f^{i}_{j}
are polynomial functions of the indicated fiber or metric variables.

Moreover, each term in (
9.79
) satisfies the pointwise decay bounds (for
|u|
large)

\big|\text{(each algebraic term in the first line)}\big|\;\lesssim\;\Gamma\,|u|^{-2}\cdot\mathcal{P}(p^{3},p^{4},\not{p}),

and every term involving a momentum integral is bounded by

\big|\text{(each integral term)}\big|\;\lesssim\;\Gamma\,|u|^{-2}\cdot\mathcal{Q}(p^{3},p^{4},\not{p}),

where
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
are explicit polynomial factors in the fiber variables which are uniformly integrable in
p
given the decay of
f
on the mass shell. In particular, all terms on the right-hand side of (
9.79
) are
u
-integrable and therefore do not cause loss of regularity in the energy estimates.

Differentiate the connection coefficients in antisymmetric combinations and commute derivatives exactly as in the introduction. For example, for fixed indices
A,\mu,\nu
,

e_{A}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu})=R^{3}{}_{\nu A\mu}-\Gamma^{\lambda}{}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{3}{}_{A\lambda}+\Gamma^{\lambda}{}_{A\nu}\Gamma^{3}{}_{\mu\lambda}+(\Gamma^{\lambda}{}_{A\mu}-\Gamma^{\lambda}{}_{\mu A})\Gamma^{3}{}_{\lambda\nu}.

The first term on the right is a curvature component and the remaining terms are quadratic in the connection coefficients. Using the Ricci decomposition of
R
into Weyl plus trace parts, the curvature term is expressed as a combination of Weyl components
\Psi
and matter stress-energy (which yields the momentum integrals
\int_{P}f\cdot\,
). This produces the schematic factor
f^{i}_{2}(\Psi)
and the momentum-integral terms appearing in (
9.79
).

Expanding the quadratic connection terms and collecting factors yields finite sums of products, each of which is a polynomial in the Ricci coefficients
\psi
, possibly multiplied by smooth functions of
\not{g},\not{g}^{-1},\not{p},p^{4}
. This produces the first line of (
9.79
).

The matter terms arising from the Ricci contraction of
R
contribute expressions of the form (schematically)

\int_{P}\Phi(\psi,\not{g})\,f(p)\,\mathfrak{m}(p)\,dp,

where
\mathfrak{m}(p)=\sqrt{\det\not{g}}\,p^{-3}
is the geometric fibre measure and
\Phi
depends smoothly on
\psi
and
\not{g}
. After pulling the fibre factors outside and expanding in
\not{p},p^{3},p^{4}
one obtains the integrals that appear in the second and third lines of (
9.79
).

The potentially non-integrable expression,

-p^{3}e_{A}(b^{B})+\Gamma^{B}{}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}+\frac{p^{3}}{|u|}\delta^{B}_{A},

is algebraically rearranged by expanding
\Gamma^{B}{}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}
into its frame components:

\Gamma^{B}{}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}=\Gamma^{B}{}_{A4}p^{4}+\not{\Gamma}^{B}{}_{AC}p^{C}+\Gamma^{B}{}_{A3}p^{3}.

Recalling the definition

\mathcal{G}^{A}{}_{B}:=\Gamma^{A}{}_{3B}+e_{A}(b^{B})\quad\text{(equivalently }\Gamma^{A}{}_{3B}=\Gamma^{A}{}_{B3}-e_{A}(b^{B})\text{)},

one obtains the identity

-p^{3}e_{A}(b^{B})+\Gamma^{B}{}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}+\frac{p^{3}}{|u|}\delta^{B}_{A}=\Gamma^{B}{}_{A4}p^{4}+\not{\Gamma}^{B}{}_{AC}p^{C}+\widehat{\mathcal{G}}^{B}{}_{A}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(\operatorname{tr}\mathcal{G}+\tfrac{2}{|u|}\Big)\delta^{B}_{A},

where
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}=\mathcal{G}-\tfrac{1}{2}(\operatorname{tr}\mathcal{G})\,\mathrm{Id}
denotes the trace-free part of
\mathcal{G}
.

By the bootstrap/pointwise estimates on the Ricci coefficients, one has

\big|\operatorname{tr}\mathcal{G}+\tfrac{2}{|u|}\big|\;\lesssim\;\Gamma\,|u|^{-2}.

Thus the dangerous
\tfrac{p^{3}}{|u|}
contribution is exactly cancelled, leaving only
\lesssim\Gamma|u|^{-2}
. The remaining terms inherit at least
|u|^{-2}
decay from the bootstrap hypotheses. Therefore, every term is
u
-integrable and does not produce the non-integrable obstruction originally feared.

Collecting the estimates yields the decomposition (
9.79
) together with the stated decay bounds. Consequently, the coefficient
C^{0}_{B}
is composed of finitely many algebraic polynomial-type terms and momentum-integral terms, each of which decays sufficiently rapidly in
|u|
(and is uniformly integrable in the fibre variable) to avoid loss of regularity in the subsequent energy estimates.

We need to be careful here since we can not use the scale-invariant Holder inequality in estimating the product terms, since we do not know the signature of the Vlasov momentum variables
\{p^{4},p^{3},p^{A},p^{B}\}
. Therefore, we convert the scale-invariant estimates of the Ricci coefficients and the Weyl curvature components to the unscaled form and use these to control the Vlasov norms. First, note the following for a Ricci coefficient belonging to the category
\psi_{g}

\displaystyle||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{a\Gamma}{|u|^{3}},~||\operatorname{tr}\chi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\Gamma}{|u|},~||\eta||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|^{2}}\Gamma,~||\underline{\eta}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|^{2}}\Gamma.

(9.80)

and the following pointwise estimates (of the tensor norm)

\displaystyle|\psi_{g}|\lesssim\frac{\Gamma}{|u|},~|\not{g}^{-1}|\lesssim 1,~|\underline{\hat{\chi}}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma}{|u|^{2}},~|\widetilde{\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}}|\lesssim\frac{\Gamma}{|u|^{2}},~|\hat{\chi}|\lesssim\frac{\Gamma a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|},~|p^{4}|\lesssim\frac{1}{|u|^{2}},~|p^{3}|\lesssim 1,~|\not{\Gamma}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|^{2}},

\displaystyle|\not{p}|\lesssim\frac{1}{|u|},~|\not{g}|\lesssim 1,~|f|\lesssim a,|p^{1,2}|\lesssim\frac{1}{|u|^{2}},~|\alpha|\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{R}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|},~|\beta|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{2}},~|\underline{\beta}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{4}},~|(\rho,\sigma)|\lesssim\frac{a\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{3}},~|\underline{\alpha}|\lesssim\frac{a^{2}\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{5}}.

This yields the following component-wise and point-wise estimates

\displaystyle\lvert\hat{\chi}_{AB}\rvert\lesssim\Gamma a^{\frac{1}{2}}\lvert u\rvert,\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\operatorname{tr}\chi\rvert\lesssim\frac{\Gamma}{\lvert u\rvert},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{AB}\rvert\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma,\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\rvert\lesssim\frac{\Gamma}{\lvert u\rvert},

\displaystyle\lvert{\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{A}}^{B}\rvert\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\underline{\eta}_{A}\rvert\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma}{\lvert u\rvert},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert{\hat{\chi}_{A}}^{B}\rvert\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma}{\lvert u\rvert},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert e_{A}(b^{B})\rvert\lesssim\frac{\Gamma}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},

(9.81)

\displaystyle\lvert\eta^{A}\rvert\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma}{\lvert u\rvert^{3}},\hskip 8.53581pt\lvert\omega\rvert\lesssim\frac{\Gamma}{\lvert u\rvert},|\beta_{A}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{R}}{|u|},~|\alpha_{AB}|\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|\mathcal{R},~|\underline{\beta}_{A}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{3}},

\displaystyle|(\rho,\sigma)|\lesssim\frac{a\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{3}},~|\underline{\alpha}_{AB}|\lesssim\frac{a^{2}\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{3}}

(9.82)

along with the basic estimates

\frac{c}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\leq\not{g}^{AB}(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\leq\frac{C}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}},\hskip 8.53581ptc\hskip 1.42262pt\lvert u\rvert^{2}\leq\not{g}_{AB}(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})\leq C\lvert u\rvert^{2}.

(9.83)

We will use the component-wise estimates instead of tensor norms. This is because we estimate the components of several derivatives of the Vlasov distribution function
f
. For example, we will estimate
V_{A}V_{B}f
component-wise (in the frame that we are explicitly working) and while the tensor norm of the tensor
\xi_{AB}:=\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}V_{A}V_{B}f
would be defined as follows

\displaystyle||\xi||:=\sqrt{\not{g}^{AC}\not{g}^{BD}\xi_{AB}\xi_{CD}}.

(9.84)

Using these facts, it is easy to observe that the term
\sum f^{i}_{1}(\psi)f^{i}_{2}(\Psi)f^{i}_{3}(p^{4})f^{i}_{4}(\not{p})f^{i}_{5}(\not{g})f^{i}_{6}(\not{g}^{-1})
enjoys integrable decay and more precisely verifies a bound of the following type

\displaystyle|\sum f^{i}_{1}(\psi)f^{i}_{2}(\Psi)f^{i}_{3}(p^{4})f^{i}_{4}(\not{p})f^{i}_{5}(\not{g})f^{i}_{6}(\not{g}^{-1})|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma}{|u|^{2}}

(9.85)

where we have noted the worst possible decay. In addition, the stress-energy tensor part of
C^{0}_{B}
decays faster and obeys

\displaystyle|\not{g}\not{p}\left(p^{4}\int_{p}fp^{3}dp+p^{3}\int_{p}fp^{4}dp+\not{p}\int_{p}f\not{g}\not{p}dp\right)+p^{4}p^{3}\int_{p}f\not{g}\not{p}dp+p^{3}p^{3}\int_{p}fp^{4}\not{g}\not{p}dp+\not{p}p^{3}\int_{P}f\not{g}\not{p}\not{g}\not{p}dp|

(9.86)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{a}{|u|^{4}}.

(9.87)

Therefore, the decay estimate verifies by
C^{0}_{B}
reads

\displaystyle|C^{0}_{B}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Gamma,\mathcal{R})}{|u|^{2}}.

(9.88)

We will proceed in an exactly similar manner to extract the decay estimates for the remaining error coefficients. Recall the following expression for the error coefficient
C^{B}_{A}

\displaystyle C^{B}_{A}=-p^{3}\hskip 1.42262pte_{A}(b^{B})+(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}{\delta_{A}}^{B})

(9.89)

which once again exhibits the cancellation property, yielding integrable decay as in
. Ultimately this verifies

\displaystyle|C^{B}_{A}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma}{|u|^{2}}.

(9.90)

This cancellation, resulting in an integrable decay feature, is ubiquitous in this study. Next we consider

\displaystyle C^{4}_{A}

\displaystyle=\big[-p^{3}\hskip 1.42262pte_{A}(b^{B})+\big(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}{\delta_{A}}^{B}\big)\big]\big(\frac{\frac{1}{2}\chi_{BC}p^{C}}{p^{3}}-\underline{\eta}_{B}\big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\big(\chi_{AB}p^{B}-\underline{\eta}_{A}p^{3}\big)|u|\underline{\omega}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bigg((e_{A}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu}))p^{\mu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\nu}+\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})+\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\Gamma^{C}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})-\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})\bigg)\frac{1}{p^{3}}.

(9.91)

Once again notice the appearance of the factor
-p^{3}\hskip 1.42262pte_{A}(b^{B})+\big(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu}p^{\nu}+\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}{\delta_{A}}^{B}\big)
that yields cancellation and integrable decay (here it does not even require the cancellation property since it is multiplied by a factor that forces the whole expression to be integrable-however cancellation yields an improved decay rate). The derivatives of the connection produce Riemann curvature as promised

\displaystyle e_{A}(\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{3}_{A\nu})=R^{3}~_{\nu A\mu}-\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}+\Gamma^{\lambda}_{A\nu}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\lambda}+(\Gamma^{\lambda}_{A\mu}-\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu A})\Gamma^{3}_{\lambda\nu}.

(9.92)

More explicitly, recalling the notation from the expression of
C^{0}_{A}
, we write

\displaystyle C^{4}_{A}

\displaystyle=\sum f^{i}_{1}(\psi)f^{i}_{2}(\Psi)f^{i}_{3}(p^{4})f^{i}_{4}(\not{p})f^{i}_{5}(\not{g})f^{i}_{6}(\not{g}^{-1})+\not{g}\not{p}\left(p^{4}\int_{p}fp^{3}\,dp+p^{3}\int_{p}fp^{4}\,dp+\not{p}\int_{p}f\not{g}\not{p}\,dp\right)

\displaystyle+p^{4}p^{3}\int_{p}f\not{g}\not{p}\,dp+p^{3}p^{3}\int_{p}fp^{4}\not{g}\not{p}\,dp+\not{p}p^{3}\int_{p}f\not{g}\not{p}\not{g}\not{p}\ dp.

(9.93)

Here we note an important null structure of the Weyl curvature terms appearing in

\sum f^{i}_{1}(\psi)f^{i}_{2}(\Psi)f^{i}_{3}(p^{4})f^{i}_{4}(\not{p})f^{i}_{5}(\not{g})f^{i}_{6}(\not{g}^{-1}).

These terms read

\displaystyle\underline{\beta}_{A}p^{3}p^{3}+\beta_{A}p^{4}p^{3}+\beta_{A}\not{p}_{C}\not{p}^{C}+\alpha_{AB}p^{4}\not{p}^{B}+\underline{\alpha}_{AB}\not{p}^{B}p^{3}.

(9.94)

The components
\alpha_{AB}
are the most dangerous terms since they behave like
a^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|
. But note that it always appears multiplied by
p^{4}
and
\not{p}^{B}
both of which decay like
|u|^{-2}
. On the other hand,
p^{3}
does appear with
\underline{\beta}
and
\underline{\alpha}
, but they exhibit superior integrable decay by themselves. In summary, the connection/curvature components with less decay always appear with
p^{4}
and/or
\not{p}^{A}
while the dangerous momentum components
p^{3}
(that enjoys no decay) appear with connection/curvature components with superior integrable decay. Every term, therefore, enjoys integrability. This is crucial. Stress-energy tensor components exhibit integrable decay as well. More precisely,

\displaystyle\big|\not{g}\not{p}\left(p^{4}\int_{p}fp^{3}\,dp+p^{3}\int_{p}fp^{4}\,dp+\not{p}\int_{p}f\not{g}\not{p}\,dp\right)+p^{4}p^{3}\int_{p}f\not{g}\not{p}\,dp+p^{3}p^{3}\int_{p}fp^{4}\not{g}\not{p}\,dp+\not{p}p^{3}\int_{p}f\not{g}\not{p}\not{g}\not{p}\ dp\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{a}{|u|^{4}}

(9.95)

and
C^{4}_{A}
verifies

\displaystyle|C^{4}_{A}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{3}}+\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma}{|u|^{2}}.

(9.96)

Now note the next coefficient is
C^{4+B}_{A}
. We write the complete expression because we want to point out a few vital cancellations that are responsible for yielding integrable decay. Direct computations yield

\displaystyle C^{4+B}_{A}

\displaystyle=|u|^{2}\,\chi^{B}_{A}\Big(\underline{\omega}\,p^{4}p^{3}+\underline{\eta}\,p^{4}p^{4}+\underline{\chi}_{CD}p^{C}p^{D}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+|u|^{2}\Big(\underline{\eta}_{A}p^{A}p^{3}+\eta_{A}p^{A}p^{3}+\chi_{CD}p^{C}p^{D}\Big)\underline{\chi}^{B}_{A}

\displaystyle\quad+|u|^{2}\Big(\underline{\eta}^{C}p^{4}p^{3}+\eta^{C}p^{4}p^{3}+2\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}p^{A}p^{3}+\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{EF}p^{E}p^{F}\Big)\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{AC}

\displaystyle\quad+|u|^{2}\Big(\underline{\eta}_{A}\eta^{B}p^{4}p^{3}+\underline{\eta}_{A}\chi^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{C}+\underline{\chi}_{AC}\eta^{B}p^{C}p^{3}+\underline{\chi}_{AC}\chi^{B}_{D}p^{C}p^{D}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+|u|^{2}\Big(\underline{\eta}_{A}\underline{\eta}^{B}p^{4}p^{3}+\chi_{AC}\underline{\eta}^{B}p^{4}p^{C}+\underline{\eta}_{A}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}p^{C}p^{3}+\chi_{AC}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{D}p^{C}p^{D}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+|u|^{2}\Big(\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}p^{3}p^{3}+\chi^{C}_{A}\chi^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{4}+\chi^{C}_{A}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{3}+\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}\chi^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{3}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+|u|^{2}\Big(\chi^{C}_{A}\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{DC}p^{4}p^{D}+\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AD}\chi^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{D}+\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{DC}p^{D}p^{C}+\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AD}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}p^{D}p^{C}+\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AD}\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{EC}p^{E}p^{D}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+|u|^{2}\Bigg[\underline{\eta}_{A}\underline{\eta}^{B}p^{4}p^{3}+\underline{\eta}_{A}\chi^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{C}+\underline{\chi}_{AC}\chi^{B}_{D}p^{C}p^{D}+\underline{\eta}_{A}\eta^{B}p^{4}p^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\underline{\eta}_{A}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}p^{C}p^{3}+\chi_{AC}\eta^{B}p^{4}p^{C}+\chi_{AC}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{D}p^{C}p^{D}+\chi^{C}_{A}\chi^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{4}+\chi^{C}_{A}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\underbrace{\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}p^{3}p^{3}}_{V}+\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{CD}p^{D}p^{3}+\chi^{C}_{A}\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{CD}p^{4}p^{D}+\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AD}\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{CE}p^{E}p^{D}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\underline{\eta}_{A}\underline{\eta}^{B}p^{4}p^{3}+\underline{\eta}_{A}\big(\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}-e_{C}(b^{B})\big)p^{4}p^{C}+\underline{\chi}_{CA}\underline{\eta}^{B}p^{C}p^{3}+\underline{\chi}_{CA}\chi^{B}_{D}p^{C}p^{D}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\eta_{A}\eta^{B}p^{4}p^{3}+\eta^{B}\chi_{CA}p^{4}p^{C}+\eta_{A}\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}p^{C}p^{3}+\chi_{CA}\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{CD}p^{C}p^{D}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\big(\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}-e_{A}(b^{C})\big)\chi^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{4}+\big(\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}-e_{A}(b^{C})\big)\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{3}+\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}\chi^{B}_{C}p^{4}p^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\big(\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}-e_{A}(b^{C})\big)\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{CD}p^{4}p^{D}+\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{CD}p^{D}p^{3}+\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{DA}\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{CE}p^{E}p^{D}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\Big(\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{AC}+\tfrac{\not{g}_{CD}p^{D}}{2p^{3}}\chi^{B}_{A}\Big)\Big(2(\underline{\eta}^{C}+\eta^{C})p^{4}p^{3}+\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AB}p^{A}p^{B}+2\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}p^{A}p^{3}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\Big(\underline{\chi}^{B}_{A}-\tfrac{p^{4}}{p^{3}}\chi^{B}_{A}\Big)\Big(\underline{\eta}_{A}p^{4}p^{A}+\zeta_{A}p^{4}p^{A}+(\eta_{A}-\underline{\eta}_{A})p^{A}p^{3}+\chi_{AB}p^{A}p^{B}+\underline{\omega}p^{4}p^{3}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\Big(\chi^{C}_{A}p^{4}+\underbrace{\underline{\chi}^{C}_{A}p^{3}}_{I}+\not{\Gamma}^{C}_{AB}p^{B}\Big)\Big(\chi^{B}_{C}p^{4}+\underbrace{(\underline{\chi}^{B}_{C}-e_{C}(b^{B}))p^{3}}_{II}+\not{\Gamma}^{B}_{DC}p^{D}+\tfrac{\not{g}_{CD}p^{D}}{2p^{3}}\big(\underline{\eta}^{B}p^{3}+\eta^{B}p^{3}+\chi^{B}_{C}p^{C}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\underbrace{\tfrac{p^{3}}{|u|}}_{III}\Big(\chi^{B}_{A}p^{4}+\underbrace{\underline{\chi}^{B}_{A}p^{3}}_{IV}+\tfrac{\not{g}_{AD}p^{D}}{2p^{3}}\big(\underline{\eta}^{B}p^{3}+\eta^{B}p^{3}+\chi^{B}_{C}\big)\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\bigg({(e_{A}(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu}))\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\mu}p^{\nu}}+\Gamma^{C}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\mu}\hskip 1.42262ptp^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\lambda})\Gamma^{B}_{A\lambda}+\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\lambda})\Gamma^{B}_{A\lambda}

\displaystyle{-{\Gamma^{C}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{C}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})}}-\Gamma^{3}_{A\lambda}p^{\lambda}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\overline{p}^{3}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})+\big({-\frac{(p^{3})^{2}}{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}{\delta_{A}}^{B}}+\frac{{\delta_{A}}^{B}}{\lvert u\rvert}\Gamma^{3}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\big)

\displaystyle-{\frac{p^{3}}{\lvert u\rvert}\partial_{\overline{p}^{A}}(p^{\mu}p^{\nu})\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu}}\bigg)\hskip 1.42262pt\frac{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}{p^{3}}.

We first emphasize a crucial structural feature of the expression for
C^{4+B}_{A}
.
In the expansions above, the most singular contributions arise from terms quadratic in

\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}
, specifically of the schematic form
\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}\cdot\operatorname{tr}\underline{\chi}
.
These appear within the combinations labeled
I,II,III,IV
, and
V
.
A direct inspection reveals that these contributions cancel
exactly
.
This cancellation is indispensable: without it, one would be left with non–integrable terms
in the energy hierarchy, thereby obstructing any possibility of closing the estimates.

Next, we recall that derivatives of the connection coefficients never appear in isolation but are
always absorbed into curvature components. Indeed, for the Levi–Civita connection one has the identity

\displaystyle e_{A}(\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\nu})-e_{\mu}(\Gamma^{B}_{A\nu})

\displaystyle=R^{B}{}_{\nu A\mu}-\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{A\lambda}+\Gamma^{\lambda}_{A\nu}\Gamma^{B}_{\mu\lambda}+(\Gamma^{\lambda}_{A\mu}-\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu A})\Gamma^{B}_{\lambda\nu}.

(9.97)

Thus, apparent derivatives of connection coefficients reorganize into curvature together with lower-order quadratic terms, thereby precluding any loss of derivatives.

In the context of the energy estimates, it is essential to control the contribution of
C^{4+B}_{A}
